Approximating the signature of Brownian motion for high order SDE simulation
James Foster

Abstract
The signature is a collection of iterated integrals describing the “shape” of a path. It appears naturally in the Taylor expansions of controlled differential equations and, as a consequence, is arguably the central object within rough path theory. In this paper, we will consider the signature of Brownian motion with time, and present both new and recently developed approximations for some of its integrals. Since these integrals (or equivalent Lévy areas) are nonlinear functions of the Brownian path, they are not Gaussian and known to be challenging to simulate. To conclude the paper, we will present some applications of these approximations to the high order numerical simulation of stochastic differential equations (SDEs).



MSC Classification]60H35, 60J65, 60L90, 65C30

keywords
Brownian motion, numerical methods, stochastic differential equations, Lévy area, path signature

[

1
Introduction

Since its development at the turn of the 20th century
Bachelier (1900)
;
Einstein (1905)
, Brownian motion has seen widespread application for modelling real-world time-evolving random phenomena
Mazo (2002)
.
In particular, Brownian motion
W=\{W_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is commonly used as the source of continuous-time random noise for stochastic differential equations (SDEs) of the form

\displaystyle dy_{t}=f(y_{t})\hskip 0.7113ptdt+\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}(y_{t})\hskip 0.7113ptdW_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(1)

where
W=(W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,W_{d})
is a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion and
f,g_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{e}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{e}
denote vector fields on
\mathbb{R}^{e}
that are sufficiently regular so that a solution to (
1
) exists.
The SDE (
1
) can understood using either Itô or Stratonovich stochastic integration and we refer the reader to
Øksendal (2010)
for an introduction to stochastic calculus and SDE theory.

Given an SDE, it is natural to understand the well-posedness of the solution map:

\displaystyle\big(y_{0}\hskip 0.7113pt,W\big)\mapsto\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(2)

For much of the 20th century, the continuity of (
2
) remained a mystery when the underlying Brownian motion was multidimensional and
g=(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d})
was generic.
However, this was eventually resolved in 1998 by Lyons’ theory of rough paths
Lyons (1998)
which showed that the aptly named “Itô-Lyons” map (
2
) does become continuous when the driving Brownian path is defined along with its (second) iterated integrals.

Rough path theory would then have a notable impact in stochastic analysis
Friz and
Hairer (2020)
;
Friz and
Victoir (2010)
as well as data science (where, the driving signal
X
comes from sequential data
Kidger et al. (2020)
);
and this collection of iterated integrals would become known as the “signature”
Hambly and Lyons (2010)
;
Morrill et al. (2021)
.

Definition 1

(Signature of a path
Hambly and Lyons (2010)
;
Morrill et al. (2021)
)
.

Let
X:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a continuous path. Then, for
N\geq 1
, the depth-
N
signature of
X
over the interval
[a,b]
is defined as

\displaystyle S_{a,b}(X)=\Big(1,\big\{S_{a,b}^{\hskip 0.7113pti}(X)\big\}_{1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd}\hskip 0.7113pt,\big\{S_{a,b}^{\hskip 0.7113pti\hskip 0.7113pt,j}(X)\big\}_{1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,\big\{S_{a,b}^{\hskip 0.7113pti_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113pt\cdots,i_{N}}(X)\big\}_{1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113pt\cdots,\hskip 0.7113pti_{N}\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd}\Big),

where each term
\hskip 0.7113ptS_{a,b}^{\hskip 0.7113pti_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,i_{n}}(X)
is given by

\displaystyle S_{a,b}^{\hskip 0.7113pti_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,i_{n}}(X):=\idotsint\displaylimits_{a\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptu_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pt\cdots\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptu_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptb}dX_{u_{1}}^{i_{1}}\cdots dX_{u_{n}}^{i_{n}}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(3)

Remark 1
.

When
X
has bounded variation (or length), then the iterated integrals (
3
) can be defined by Riemann-Stieltjes integration. However, this is no longer the case for Brownian motion and (
3
) is usually defined by either Itô or Stratonovich integration.

The signature of Brownian motion is especially important in the study of numerical methods for SDEs since iterated integrals naturally appear within Taylor expansions (we give Theorem
1
as such an example). As a consequence, numerical methods that use iterated integrals can achieve higher order rates of convergence. On the other hand, Clark and Cameron
Clark and
Cameron (1980)
showed that methods which use only values of the Brownian path are limited to a strong convergence rate of just
O(\sqrt{h}\hskip 0.7113pt)
, where
h
is the step size.

Theorem 1

(Stochastic Itô-Taylor expansion
(
Milstein and
Tretyakov, 2004
, Equation (2.10) and Lemma 2.2.)
)
.

Consider the Itô SDE (
1
) where the vector fields
f,g_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{e}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{e}
are assumed to be sufficiently smooth and with linear growth (see condition (2.17) in
(
Milstein and
Tretyakov, 2004
, Lemma 2.2.)
).
Then the solution
y
of (
1
) can be expressed over the interval
[s,t]
, with
0\leq s\leq t
, as

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=y_{s}+f(y_{s})\hskip 0.7113pth+\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}(y_{s})W_{s,t}^{i}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}g_{j}^{\prime}(y_{s})g_{i}(y_{s})\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dW_{v}^{i}\,dW_{u}^{j}+R_{s,t}\,,

(4)

where
h:=t-s
,
W_{s,t}^{i}:=W_{t}^{i}-W_{s}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt
and the remainder term
R_{s,t}
is given by

\displaystyle R_{s,t}

\displaystyle:=\int_{s}^{t}\big(f(y_{u})-f(y_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113ptdu+\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\big(\hskip 0.7113ptg_{j}^{\prime}(y_{v})g_{i}(y_{v})-g_{j}^{\prime}(y_{s})g_{i}(y_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113ptdW_{v}^{i}\,dW_{u}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}g_{i}^{\prime}(y_{v})f(y_{v})\,dv\,dW_{u}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\Delta g_{i}(y_{v})\,dv\,dW_{u}^{i}\,,

with
\,\mathbb{E}\big[\|R_{s,t}\|^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\big(1+\mathbb{E}\big[\|y_{s}\|^{2}\big]\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}h^{\frac{3}{2}}
for some constant
C
depending on
f
and
g_{i}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Remark 2
.

When the vector fields
g:=(g_{1},\cdots,g_{d})
satisfy a commutativity condition,

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912ptg_{i}^{\prime}(y)g_{j}(y)=g_{j}^{\prime}(y)g_{i}(y),\hskip 28.45274pt\forall y\in\mathbb{R}^{e},

(5)

for
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
, it follows by Itô’s lemma that the Taylor expansion (
4
) becomes

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=y_{s}+f(y_{s})\hskip 0.7113pth+\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}(y_{s})W_{s,t}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}g_{j}^{\prime}(y_{s})g_{i}(y_{s})\big(W_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-\delta_{ij}h\big)+R_{s,t}\,,

where
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta. In this case, we can further expand
R_{s,t}
in terms of other integrals in the signature of Brownian motion coupled with time
\overline{W}_{t}:=\{(t,W_{t})\}
.
In particular, the integrals
\big\{\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}W_{t_{k}\hskip 0.7113pt,u}\,du\big\}
can be used alongside path increments
\{W_{t_{k}\hskip 0.7113pt,t_{k+1}}\}
by numerical methods to achieve faster rates of strong convergence
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Tang and Xiao (2019)
.

In this paper, we will consider the signature of “space-time” Brownian motion
\overline{W}:=\{(t,W_{t})\}_{0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptt\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptT}\hskip 0.7113pt,
due to its importance within the numerical analysis of SDEs.
We shall present some new and recent approximations for the non-Gaussian integrals (
6
) and (
7
) which can then be used by SDE solvers to achieve high order convergence.

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dW_{v}^{i}\,dW_{u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(6)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdu,

(7)

where
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
and
W_{s,u}:=W_{u}-W_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt
. As indicated in Remark
2
, the iterated integral (
6
) is important for general SDEs whereas (
7
) is more relevant to SDEs where the noise is scalar (
d=1
), additive (
g^{\prime}=0
) or otherwise commutative (equation (
5
)).

Unsurprisingly, these integrals and their approximation theory are well studied.
For example, due to Itô’s lemma (or integration by parts in the Stratovonich setting), iterated integrals of Brownian motion have a well-known algebraic structure
Gaines (1994)
;
Li and Liu (1997)
.
In particular, once the increment
W_{s,t}
has been generated, the integral (
6
) can be expressed in terms of a “Lévy area”, which was introduced by Paul Lévy in 1940
Lévy (1940)
.
Nevertheless, simulating such integrals is a challenging problem and an algorithm for exactly generating Lévy area (given a path increment) is only known for
d=2

Gaines and Lyons (1994)
.
Moreover, it was shown that any approximation using
N
Gaussian random variables (obtained by linear maps on the Brownian path) converges slowly at rate
O(N^{-\frac{1}{2}})

Dickinson (2007)
.

Definition 2

(Lévy area)
.

Over an interval
[s,t]
, we will define the (space-space) Lévy area of Brownian motion as a
d\times d
matrix
A_{s,t}
whose
(i,j)
-th entry is given by

\displaystyle A_{s,t}^{i,j}:=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}\,dW_{u}^{j}-\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdW_{u}^{i}\bigg),

(8)

where
W_{s,u}:=W_{u}-W_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Figure 1:
The (space-space) Lévy area is the chordal area enclosed by independent Brownian motions (diagram taken from
Foster (2020)
).

Despite this slow convergence, several strong approximations have been proposed
Foster and
Habermann (2023)
;
Kloeden and
Platen (1992)
;
Milstein (1994)
;
Milstein (1988)
;
Kloeden
et al. (1992)
;
Kuznetsov (1997)
;
Kuznetsov (2019)
and we refer the reader to
(
Foster and
Habermann, 2023
, Table 2)
for a summary of these approaches.
By including additional Gaussian random variables so that approximations have the same covariance as Lévy area and by constructing non-trivial probabilistic couplings, it is possible to achieve faster
O(N^{-1})
convergence rates in the 2-Wasserstein metric.
Examples of Lévy area approximations that allow for this analysis include those of Wiktorsson
Wiktorsson (2001)
, Rydén & Wiktorsson
Rydén and
Wiktorsson (2001)
, Davie
Davie (2014)
;
Flint and Lyons (2016)
, Mrongowius & Rößler
Mrongowius and
Rößler (2022)
and Foster
(
Foster, 2020
, Chapter 7)
. When
d=2
, Malham & Wiese
Malham and Wiese (2014)
give a highly accurate Lévy area approximation based on an expansion involving logistic random variables. However, there have also been papers introducing high order methodologies for SDEs (including techniques for variance reduction) which avoid Lévy area generation
Cruzeiro et al. (2004)
;
Giles and Szpruch (2014)
;
Iguchi et al. (2025)
.

Recently, a machine learning (ML) approach was taken to train a neural network to generate samples that are close to Brownian Lévy area in a Wasserstein sense
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
. While the ML model empirically outperformed the standard methods discussed above, it is a “black box” and thus unlikely to have a theoretical
O(N^{-1})
convergence rate.
However, the experiments in
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
also revealed that the weak approximation proposed in
(
Foster, 2020
, Definition 7.3.5)
, which aims to match the first four moments of Lévy area, gave similar 2-Wasserstein and maximum mean discrepancy errors as the ML model.
Therefore, in section
3
, we will particularly focus on this moment-based approximation.

When the noise vector fields
\{g_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,g_{d}\}
satisfy the commutativity condition (
5
), the (space-space) Lévy area will no longer appear in the Taylor expansion of the SDE. In this case, it becomes more important to study approximations of the integral (
7
).
Starting with the work of Clark
Clark (1982)
as well as Newton
Newton (1991)
and Castell & Gaines
Castell and
Gaines (1996)
, the concept of “asymptotically efficient” numerical methods for SDEs was developed.

Methods are asymptotically efficient if they use the following approximation of (
7
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}du\,\Big|\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}\bigg]=\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113pth\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{6}h^{2}\delta_{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(9)

Since the above is a conditional expectation, it follows that the right-hand side of (
9
) gives the least
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
error among the
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
-measurable estimators of the integral (
7
).
However, despite this notion of optimality, Tang & Xiao
Tang and Xiao (2019)
developed an estimator for (
7
) which is “asymptotically optimal” (i.e. approaches an optimal rate as
N\rightarrow\infty
).
Foster
Foster (2020)
;
Foster et al. (2024)
argued that asymptotically efficient methods could be improved using

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}du\,\Big|\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t},\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu\bigg]=\frac{1}{3}hW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{6}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{15}h^{2}\delta_{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(10)

where
H_{s,t}:=\frac{1}{h}\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu-\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{h}{12}I_{d})
is independent of
W_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{d})
.

The estimator (
10
) is particularly appealing as it is both optimal in an
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
sense and uses
\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu
, which is often needed by high order numerical methods for SDEs
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
.
In section
4
, we will consider some additional random vectors related to the Brownian motion
and present the associated conditional expectations. These are more accurate than (
10
) and have recently been applied to improve high order splitting methods
Foster et al. (2024)
.

The paper is outlined as follows. In section
2
, we define the integrals in the depth-
3
Brownian signature which are normally distributed and can thus be generated exactly. These will then serve as input random vectors in subsequent numerical approximations.

In section
3
, we will consider the problem of generating (space-space) Lévy area and present the moment-matching approximation proposed in the thesis
(
Foster, 2020
, Section 7.3)
.
Moreover, we shall introduce a random variable on
\{-1,1\}^{4}
so that the fourth moment
\mathbb{E}\big[A_{s,t}^{ij}A_{s,t}^{jk}A_{s,t}^{jk}A_{s,t}^{kl}\big]=\frac{1}{48}h^{4}
(distinct
i,j,k,l
) is better matched by the approximation.

In section
4
, we will turn our attention to “space-space-time” Lévy area, which is equivalent to the integral (
7
), and establish the optimal estimator (
10
) from
Foster (2020)
;
Foster et al. (2024)
. Furthermore, we introduce a random vector
n_{s,t}
defined by
n_{s,t}^{i}:=\mathrm{Sgn}(H_{s,u}^{i}-H_{u,t}^{i})
where
u=\frac{1}{2}(s+t)
and derive a formula for the corresponding conditional expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\big(W_{s,r}^{i}\big)^{2}dr\,\Big|\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t},H_{s,t},n_{s,t}\bigg]=\frac{1}{3}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+hW_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{i}+\frac{6}{5}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{15}h^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\sqrt{6\pi}}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{3}{2}}W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(11)

The estimator (
11
) has already been used to improve high order splitting methods
Foster et al. (2024)
.

In section
5
, we give several numerical examples from the literature to demonstrate the improvements in accuracy achievable using the proposed integral approximations. In particular, the SDE solvers that we obtain outperform standard numerical methods.
Finally, in section
6
, we shall conclude the paper and suggest a future research topic.

2
Gaussian iterated integrals of Brownian motion

In this section, we will consider the iterated integrals of Brownian motion with time in the depth-3 signature that are normally distributed. Over
[s,t]
, these integrals are

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}dW_{u}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dW_{v}\,du,\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dv\,dW_{u}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dW_{r}\,dv\,du\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dW_{v}\,du\hskip 0.7113pt,\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dv\,dW_{u}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since the above integrals have some interdependencies, they can be generated using three Gaussian random vectors – which we shall now present in definitions
3
,
4
and
5
.
These quantities are especially useful for SDE solvers as they can be generated exactly.

Throughout this paper,
W=(W^{1},\cdots,W^{d})
will denote a standard
d
-dimensional Brownian motion,
I_{d}
is the
d\times d
identity matrix and we set
h:=t-s
with
0\leq s\leq t
.

Definition 3

(Path increment of Brownian motion)
.

Over an interval
[s,t]
, we define the path increment of
W
as
W_{s,t}:=W_{t}-W_{s}
. Then, by definition,
W_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{d})
.

Definition 4

(Space-time Lévy area)
.

Over an interval
[s,t]
, we define the (rescaled) space-time Lévy area of Brownian motion as

\displaystyle H_{s,t}:=\frac{1}{h}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptdu.

(12)

Remark 3
.

Since
H_{s,t}
only depends on the Brownian bridge
\big\{W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\big\}_{u\in[s,t]}\hskip 0.7113pt
,

it follows that
H_{s,t}
is independent of
W_{s,t}
. In addition, we have
H_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{12}hI_{d})
.

This distribution can be shown using a polynomial expansion of Brownian motion
Foster et al. (2024)
.

Figure 2:
H_{s,t}
is the area enclosed by a Brownian path and its linear interpolant (diagram from
Foster et al. (2024)
).

Definition 5

(Space-time-time Lévy area)
.

Over an interval
[s,t]
, we define the (rescaled) space-time-time Lévy area of Brownian motion as

\displaystyle K_{s,t}:=\frac{1}{h^{2}}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\Big)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}h-(u-s)\bigg)\hskip 0.7113ptdu.

(13)

Remark 4
.

Since
\frac{1}{2}h-(u-s)
is an odd function around
s+\frac{1}{2}h
, it directly follows that

\displaystyle K_{s,t}=\frac{1}{h^{2}}\int_{s}^{t}\underbrace{\Big(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}-\frac{6(u-s)(t-u)}{h^{2}}H_{s,t}\Big)}_{=:\,\,Z_{s,u}\text{ and called the ``Brownian arch''}.}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}h-(u-s)\bigg)\hskip 0.7113ptdu.

In
Foster et al. (2024)
, the Brownian arch was introduced and shown to be independent of
\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)
.

Hence
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}
and
K_{s,t}
are jointly independent. Similar to Remark
3
, we can use

the polynomial expansion of Brownian motion to show that
\hskip 0.7113ptK_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{720}hI_{d})

Foster (2020)
.

Figure 3:
Space-time-time Lévy area corresponds to how asymmetric in time the Brownian motion is.

As previously discussed in the introduction, the increment
W_{s,t}
and space-time Lévy area
H_{s,t}
can be used by high order solvers for SDEs satisfying the condition (
5
).
Since incorporating “space-time-time” Lévy area would not ordinarily improve these
O(h^{\frac{3}{2}})
convergence rates,
we will consider
K_{s,t}
with two specific applications in mind:

•

K_{s,t}
is needed by the approximation of (space-space) Lévy area detailed in section
3
.

•

Underdamped Langevin dynamics is fundamental model in statistical physics
Leimkuhler and
Matthews (2015)
which has seen a number of recent applications in data science
Chen et al. (2014)
;
Cheng et al. (2018)
;
Nemeth and Fearnhead (2021)
;
Chada et al. (2024)
. It is given by,

\displaystyle dx_{t}

\displaystyle=v_{t}\hskip 0.7113ptdt,

(14)

\displaystyle dv_{t}

\displaystyle=-\gamma v_{t}\hskip 0.7113ptdt-\nabla f(x_{t})\hskip 0.7113ptdt+\sqrt{2\gamma}\,dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

where
x_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\gamma>0
,
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
W
is a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion.
In
Foster et al. (2021)
;
Scott et al. (2025)
, approximations were proposed for (
14
) which uses
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t},K_{s,t})
and achieves third order convergence (provided the gradient
\nabla f
is sufficiently smooth).

We now show how the random vectors
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}
and
K_{s,t}
can be used to determine the integrals in the Brownian signature that were given at the beginning of section
2
.

Theorem 2

(Relationship between
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t})
and the Brownian signature)
.

Let
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,
and
K_{s,t}
be given by definitions
3
,
4
and
5
. Then for
0\leq s\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}dW_{u}

\displaystyle=W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(15)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dW_{v}\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,t}+hH_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(16)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dv\,dW_{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,t}-hH_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(17)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dW_{r}\,dv\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}H_{s,t}+h^{2}K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(18)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dW_{v}\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}h^{2}W_{s,t}-2h^{2}K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(19)

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dv\,dW_{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}h^{2}W_{s,t}-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}H_{s,t}+h^{2}K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(20)

Proof.

Equation (
15
) follows immediately from the definition of Itô integrals and
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
. From the definition of space-time Lévy area
H_{s,t}
, it is straightforward to show (
16
) as

\displaystyle hH_{s,t}=\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptdu=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dW_{v}\,du-\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Equation (
17
) can now be seen by applying the standard integration by parts formula,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dW_{v}\,du+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dv\,dW_{u}=hW_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(21)

To derive the remaining formulae, we first expand the integral in the definition of
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

\displaystyle h^{2}K_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\Big)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}h-(u-s)\bigg)\hskip 0.7113ptdu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}h\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\,du-\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu+\int_{s}^{t}\Big((u-s)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(u-s)\Big)W_{s,t}\,du

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}hH_{s,t}-\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu+\frac{1}{12}h^{2}W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

From the above, we arrive at the following integral identity involving
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}hH_{s,t}-h^{2}K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(22)

We will now express the remaining iterated integrals in terms of the integral (
22
).
Using integration by parts, and integrating equation (
21
) with respect to
t
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\,d((u-s)^{2})+\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)^{2}dW_{u}

\displaystyle=h^{2}W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\bigg(\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}\,dv\bigg)du+\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)\,d\bigg(\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}\,dv\bigg)

\displaystyle=h\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dW_{r}\,dv\,du+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dW_{v}\,du

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\,du.

Simplifying and rearranging these equations gives

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dv\,dW_{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}h^{2}W_{s,t}-\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\,du,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}\,dv\,du

\displaystyle=h\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu-\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\,du,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dW_{v}\,du

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}(u-s)W_{s,u}\,du-\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dW_{r}\,dv\,du.

Substituting (
22
) into the above equations gives (
18
), (
19
) and (
20
) as required.
∎

Remark 5
.

From equations (
15
) to (
20
), we see that the vectors
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}
are simply rescaled coefficients in the log-signature of space-time Brownian motion
\overline{W}_{t}:=\{(t,W_{t})\}
. The definition of a path log-signature is given in
(
Lyons et al., 2007
, Section 2.2.4)
.

Remark 6
.

By Itô’s isometry, we can compute the covariance of the below integrals,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\int_{s}^{t}dW_{u}\\[2.0pt]
\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}dv\,dW_{u}\\[2.0pt]
\hskip 0.7113pt\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{v}dr\,dv\,dW_{u}\hskip 0.7113pt\end{pmatrix}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{pmatrix}h&\frac{1}{2}h^{2}&\frac{1}{6}h^{3}\\[4.0pt]
\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{2}h^{2}&\frac{1}{3}h^{3}&\frac{1}{8}h^{4}\\[4.0pt]
\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{6}h^{3}&\frac{1}{8}h^{4}&\frac{1}{20}h^{5}\end{pmatrix}\right).

From the above with equations (
15
), (
16
) and (
20
), it is straightforward to show that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}W_{s,t}\\[2.0pt]
H_{s,t}\\[2.0pt]
K_{s,t}\end{pmatrix}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{pmatrix}h&0&0\\[1.0pt]
0&\frac{1}{12}h&0\\[1.0pt]
0&0&\frac{1}{720}h\end{pmatrix}\right).

Since the iterated integrals considered in this section are all normally distributed, it is also possible to generate them for use by SDE solvers with adaptive step sizes.
This is detailed in
Foster (2020)
;
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
and requires one to identify the conditional distributions,

\displaystyle W_{s,u}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\quad\big(W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\big)\,|\,\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big),\quad\big(W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,u}\big)\,|\,\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\big),

where
u\in[s,t]
. Whilst the distributions
W_{s,u}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,
and
\big(W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\big)\,|\,\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)

are well known, the third distribution was only recently computed explicitly
Foster (2020)
;
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
.

3
Lévy area of multidimensional Brownian motion

In this section, we shall present a moment-based weak approximation of Lévy area,
which was introduced in
Foster (2020)
and subsequently tested in
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
. At the end of the section, we will propose a new modification in the
d=4
case that better matches moments.
Recall that the Lévy area of a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion over an interval
[s,t]
is

\displaystyle A_{s,t}:=\bigg\{\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}\,dW_{u}^{j}-\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdW_{u}^{i}\bigg)\bigg\}_{1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd}.

(23)

We first note that the integral (
6
) can be decomposed into a symmetric component (depending on the increment
W_{s,t}
) and an antisymmetric component (given by (
23
)).

Theorem 3

(Decomposition of iterated integral (
6
))
.

For
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}\circ dW_{u}^{j}=\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}+A_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(24)

Proof.

The result follows from the definition of
A_{s,t}
and integration by part formula.

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}\circ dW_{u}^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}\circ dW_{u}^{j}+\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{j}\circ dW_{u}^{i}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}\circ dW_{u}^{j}-\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{j}\circ dW_{u}^{i}\bigg)=\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}+A_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

as the Itô and Stratonovich integrals coincide when
W^{i}
and
W^{j}
are independent.
∎

Following
(
Foster, 2020
, Chapter 7)
, we will compute the first four moments of Lévy area. This will require the following decompositions of Lévy area involving
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
,
which we shall express more succinctly using the standard tensor product notation
\otimes\,
.

Definition 6

(Tensor product)
.

For vectors
X\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
Y\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we define the matrix
X\otimes Y\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}\hskip 0.7113pt
with entries
\hskip 0.7113pt(X\otimes Y)^{ij}=X^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptY^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt
for
\hskip 0.7113pt1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti\hskip 0.7113pt\leq n\hskip 0.7113pt
and
\hskip 0.7113pt1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptj\hskip 0.7113pt\leq m
.

Theorem 4
.

Using
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}
, we can express the Brownian Lévy area (
23
) as

\displaystyle A_{s,t}

\displaystyle=H_{s,t}\otimes W_{s,t}-W_{s,t}\otimes H_{s,t}+b_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(25)

\displaystyle A_{s,t}

\displaystyle=H_{s,t}\otimes W_{s,t}-W_{s,t}\otimes H_{s,t}+12\big(K_{s,t}\otimes H_{s,t}-H_{s,t}\otimes K_{s,t}\big)+a_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(26)

where
b_{s,t}
denotes the Lévy area of the Brownian bridge,
B_{s,u}:=W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle b_{s,t}^{ij}:=\int_{s}^{t}B_{s,u}^{i}\circ dB_{u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt,

and
a_{s,t}
is the Lévy area of the Brownian arch,
Z_{s,u}:=B_{s,u}-\frac{6(u-s)(t-u)}{h^{2}}H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
,

\displaystyle a_{s,t}^{ij}:=\int_{s}^{t}Z_{s,u}^{i}\circ dZ_{u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

By Brownian scaling, it is enough to prove the result on the interval
[0,1]
. Using the decomposition (
24
) and
W_{t}=tW_{1}+B_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle A_{0,1}^{ij}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(tW_{1}^{i}+B_{t}^{i}\big)\circ d\big(tW_{1}^{j}+B_{t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{2}W_{1}^{i}W_{1}^{j}

\displaystyle=W_{1}^{i}W_{1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}t\,dt+W_{1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}B_{t}^{i}\,dt+W_{1}^{i}\int_{0}^{1}t\,dB_{t}^{j}+\int_{0}^{1}B_{t}^{i}\circ dB_{t}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}W_{1}^{i}W_{1}^{j}

\displaystyle=H_{0,1}^{i}W_{1}^{j}-W_{1}^{i}H_{0,1}^{j}+b_{0,1}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

which gives equation (
25
) as required. Similarly, using
B_{t}=6t(1-t)H_{0,1}+Z_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle b_{0,1}^{ij}=\int_{0}^{1}B_{t}^{i}\circ dB_{t}^{j}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(6t(1-t)H_{0,1}^{i}+Z_{t}^{i}\big)\circ d\big(6t(1-t)H_{0,1}^{j}+Z_{t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle=H_{0,1}^{i}H_{0,1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}6t(1-t)\,d(6t(1-t))+H_{0,1}^{i}\int_{0}^{1}6t(1-t)\circ dZ_{t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+H_{0,1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\,d\big(6t(1-t)\big)+\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\circ dZ_{t}^{j}

\displaystyle=H_{0,1}^{i}\int_{0}^{1}6t(1-t)\circ dZ_{t}^{j}+H_{0,1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}(6-12t)Z_{t}^{i}\,dt+a_{0,1}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Note that, by Remark
4
, we have
K_{0,1}=\int_{0}^{1}\big(\frac{1}{2}-t\big)Z_{t}\,dt
. Using integration by parts then gives

\int_{0}^{1}6t(1-t)\circ dZ_{t}=-\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}\,d(6t(1-t))=-12\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{2}-t\Big)Z_{t}\,dt=-12K_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt
. The second result (
26
) now directly follows from the above decomposition of
b_{0,1}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt
.
∎

Before computing the moments of Brownian Lévy area, we note the following result.

Theorem 5

(Distribution of the Brownian bridge Lévy area)
.

For each entry in
b_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
,

\displaystyle b_{s,t}^{ij}\sim\mathrm{Logistic}\Big(0,\frac{1}{\pi}\hskip 0.7113pth\Big).

(27)

In particular, this implies that the Brownian bridge Lévy area has the moments,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}h^{2},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]=\frac{7}{240}h^{4}.

(28)

Proof.

It was shown in
Lévy (1951)
that the joint density of
x=W_{1}^{i}
,
y=W_{1}^{j}
and
z=A_{0,1}^{ij}
is

\displaystyle p(x,y,z)=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{u}{\sinh(u)}\exp\Big(-\frac{(x^{2}+y^{2})u}{2\tanh(u)}\Big)\cos(zu)du.

Setting
x=y=0
and simplifying the above integral gives the density of
\mathrm{Logistic}\big(0,\frac{1}{\pi}\big)
(see
(
Foster, 2020
, Theorem 7.0.15)
). By the natural Brownian scaling, we arrive at equation (
27
).
Since the logistic distribution has known moments, we can obtain (
28
) as required.
∎

3.1
Conditional moments of two-dimensional Lévy area

We shall now compute moments of Brownian Lévy area conditional on
W_{s,t}
and
H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
. This result was shown in the thesis
(
Foster, 2020
, Theorem 7.1.1)
, but has not yet been published.

Theorem 6

(Conditional moments of Lévy area)
.

For
1\leq i,j\leq d
with
i\neq j
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{j}W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(29)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{20}h^{2}+\frac{1}{5}h\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big),

(30)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Skew}\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=0,

(31)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Kurt}\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=3+\frac{\frac{3}{1400}h^{2}+\frac{3}{175}h\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)}{\Big(\frac{1}{20}h^{2}+\frac{1}{5}h\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big)^{2}}\,.

(32)

Proof.

Since the Brownian arch
Z
is independent of
W_{s,t}
and
H_{s,t}
(as shown in
Foster et al. (2024)
), it follows that
a_{s,t}
is independent of
W_{s,t}
and
H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
. Moreover, since
Z^{i}
and
-Z^{i}
have the same distribution, we also have that
a_{s,t}^{ij}
and
-a_{s,t}^{ij}
have the same distribution. Therefore,
\mathbb{E}\big[a_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[a_{s,t}^{ij}\big]=0
and the conditional expectation of
b_{s,t}^{ij}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[12\hskip 0.7113ptK_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-12\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+a_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=12\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}[K_{s,t}^{i}]-12\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}[K_{s,t}^{j}]+\mathbb{E}\big[a_{s,t}^{ij}\big]=0\hskip 0.7113pt,

where we used Theorem
4
in the first line. The second moment can be computed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12\hskip 0.7113ptK_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-12\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=144\hskip 0.7113pt\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]+144\hskip 0.7113pt\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+24\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{i}a_{s,t}^{ij}\big]-24\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{j}a_{s,t}^{ij}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-288\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big].

By reflecting the
j
-th coordinate of the Brownian arch, we see that
\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{i}a_{s,t}^{ij}\big]=0
. Since
K_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{720}hI_{d}\big)
, we have
\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big]=0
and the above expression becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]+\frac{1}{5}h\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big).

Since
b_{s,t}^{ij}
has variance
\frac{1}{12}h^{2}
(see Theorem
5
), taking the expectation of the above gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]+\frac{1}{30}h^{2}\implies\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]=\frac{1}{20}h^{2},

which results in equation (
30
) as required.

To compute the conditional skewness of
A_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, we note the following moments,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]

\displaystyle=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\text{by the symmetry of }a_{s,t}^{ij}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{i}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]

\displaystyle=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\text{by the symmetry of }Z^{i}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}a_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]

\displaystyle=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\text{by the symmetry of }Z^{j}\big),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}a_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]

\displaystyle=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\text{since }a_{s,t}^{ij}=-\hskip 0.7113pta_{s,t}^{ji}\big).

Therefore, by the independence of
K_{s,t}
and
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t})
, it follows from the above that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12\hskip 0.7113ptK_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-12\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]=0.

By the polynomial expansion of Brownian motion (the main result of
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Habermann (2021)
), we have

\displaystyle W_{t}=tW_{1}

\displaystyle+6t(1-t)H_{0,1}+60t(1-t)(1-2t)K_{0,1}

\displaystyle+210\hskip 0.7113ptt(1-t)\big(5(2t-1)^{2}-1\big)M_{0,1}+G_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

for
t\in[0,1]
, where
M_{0,1}:=\int_{0}^{1}(W_{t}-tW_{1})\big(\frac{1}{2}\big(t-\frac{1}{2}\big)^{2}-\frac{1}{40}\big)\hskip 0.7113ptdt\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{100800}I_{d}\big)
is

independent of
W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt
and
G=\{G_{t}\}
is independent of
W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1},M_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt
.
Moreover, it follows from the orthogonality of the polynomials in the expansion that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}t^{k}G_{t}\hskip 0.7113ptdt=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for }k\in\{0,1,2\}.

Since
G_{0}=G_{1}=0
, integration by parts implies that
\int_{0}^{1}t^{k}dG_{t}=0
for
k\in\{0,1,2,3\}
.
Letting
p_{2}(t):=60t(1-t)(1-2t)
and
p_{3}(t):=210\hskip 0.7113ptt(1-t)\big(5(2t-1)^{2}-1\big)
, we have

\displaystyle a_{0,1}^{ij}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}Z_{u}^{i}\circ dZ_{u}^{j}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\big(\hskip 0.7113ptp_{2}(t)K_{0,1}^{i}+p_{3}(t)M_{0,1}^{i}+G_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)\circ d\big(\hskip 0.7113ptp_{2}(t)K_{0,1}^{j}+p_{3}(t)M_{0,1}^{j}+G_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)

\displaystyle=K_{0,1}^{i}M_{0,1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}p_{2}(t)\,dp_{3}(t)+M_{0,1}^{i}K_{0,1}^{j}\int_{0}^{1}p_{3}(t)\,dp_{2}(t)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\int_{0}^{1}\big(\hskip 0.7113ptp_{3}(t)M_{0,1}^{i}+G_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)\circ d\big(\hskip 0.7113ptp_{3}(t)M_{0,1}^{j}+G_{t}^{j}\big)\hskip 0.7113pt,

since
\int_{0}^{1}p_{k}(t)\,dp_{k}(t)=0
for
k\in\{0,1\}
. Applying the usual Brownian scaling gives

\displaystyle a_{s,t}^{ij}

\displaystyle=720\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)+c_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(33)

where
M_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{100800}hI_{d}\big)
is given by

\displaystyle M_{s,t}:=\frac{1}{h^{3}}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\Big)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\bigg((u-s)-\frac{1}{2}h\bigg)^{2}-\frac{1}{40}h^{2}\bigg)\hskip 0.7113ptdu,

(34)

and
c_{s,t}
is the Lévy area of the “cubic Brownian bridge”,
\widetilde{Z}_{s,u}:=Z_{s,u}-p_{2}\big(\frac{u-s}{h}\big)K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle c_{s,t}^{ij}:=\int_{s}^{t}\widetilde{Z}_{s,u}^{i}\circ d\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(35)

Since
c_{s,t}
is independent of
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(720\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)+c_{s,t}^{ij}\Big)^{2}\,\Big]

\displaystyle=720^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(c_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+1440\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)c_{s,t}^{ij}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{70}h^{2}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(c_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big].

Recall that
\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]=\frac{1}{20}h^{2}
. Thus, from the above, it follows that
\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(c_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]=\frac{1}{28}h^{2}
.

This enables us to compute the following expectation, which we will use to derive (
32
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(720\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)+c_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=720^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(c_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+1440\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)c_{s,t}^{ij}\Big]

\displaystyle=720^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}\big(M_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]+720^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\big(M_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(c_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{100800}h^{3}+\frac{1}{100800}h^{3}+\frac{1}{720}h\times\frac{1}{28}h^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{11200}h^{3}.

In the above calculation, we used the independence of
K_{s,t}
and the pair
(M_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,c_{s,t})
. By reflecting the
j
-th coordinate of the Brownian arch, it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[K_{s,t}^{i}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\Big]

\displaystyle=0,\hskip 32.72066pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{3}a_{s,t}^{ij}\Big]=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[K_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=0,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{E}\Big[K_{s,t}^{i}\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}a_{s,t}^{ij}\Big]=0.

Expanding the fourth conditional moment of the Brownian bridge Lévy area gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-12H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=12^{4}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{4}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}\Big]+12^{4}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+6\times 12^{4}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+6\times 144\hskip 0.7113pt\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+6\times 144\hskip 0.7113pt\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{25}h^{2}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{4}\Big)+\frac{6}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{27}{350}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big)+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big].

Since
\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\,\Big]=\frac{7}{240}h^{4}
by Theorem
5
, taking an expectation of the above leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\,\Big]=\frac{1}{200}h^{4}+\frac{1}{600}h^{4}+\frac{9}{700}h^{4}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]\implies\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]=\frac{27}{2800}h^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Therefore, the above expansion for the fourth conditional moment of
b_{s,t}^{ij}
simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{27}{2800}h^{4}+\frac{27}{350}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{3}{25}h^{2}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)^{2},

and dividing by
\mathrm{Var}\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)^{2}=\big(\frac{1}{20}h^{2}+\frac{1}{5}h\big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\big)\big)^{2}
gives (
32
).
∎

Figure 4:
Given
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t})
, Brownian Lévy area always has more kurtosis than a normal distribution.

3.2
Conditional moments of multi-dimensional Lévy area

Continuing from the previous subsection, we now compute cross-moments when
d\geq 3
.

Theorem 7

(Additional moments of the Brownian bridge Lévy area)
.

Let
b_{s,t}
be the Brownian bridge Lévy area over
[s,t]
. Then for distinct
i,j,k,l\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{720}h^{4},

(36)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\Big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{720}h^{4},

(37)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}b_{s,t}^{kl}b_{s,t}^{li}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{720}h^{4}.

(38)

Proof.

Let
B_{s,u}:=W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}
be the Brownian bridge on
[s,t]
. Then the process

\displaystyle B^{i,k}:=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\big(B^{i}+B^{k}\big),

(39)

is also a Brownian bridge process with the same distribution as
B^{i}
(and
B^{k}
). Hence

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}B_{s,u}^{i,k}\circ B_{u}^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\int_{s}^{t}B_{s,u}^{i}\circ B_{u}^{j}+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\int_{s}^{t}B_{s,u}^{k}\circ B_{u}^{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}B_{s,u}^{i}\circ B_{u}^{j}-\int_{s}^{t}B_{s,u}^{j}\circ B_{u}^{k}\bigg)=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}-b_{s,t}^{jk}\big),

has the same distribution as
b_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt
. Therefore, by Theorem
5
, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big(b_{s,t}^{ij}-b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\Big)\bigg)^{\hskip-0.7113pt4}\,\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]=\frac{7}{240}h^{4}.

Expanding the bracket on the left-hand side gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{3}\Big]+\frac{3}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)\Big]+\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{4}\Big].

By the symmetry of
B^{i}\,
(or
B^{k}
), it follows that the second and fourth terms are zero.
Since the expectations of the first and last terms are known, we obtain equation (
36
). To calculate the next cross-moment, we use the decompositions given by Theorem
4
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 60.60437pt\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{k}-H_{s,t}^{j}K_{s,t}^{k}\big)+a_{s,t}^{jk}\big)\Big].

When the right-hand side is expanded, most of the resulting terms are zero due to the independence of
H_{s,t}
and the pair
(K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,a_{s,t})
. Evaluating the only non-zero term gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\Big]

\displaystyle=-144\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{k}\big)^{2}\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]=-\frac{1}{720}h^{3},

as required. By considering the Brownian bridge
B^{ik}=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\big(B^{i}+B^{k}\big)
, we can see that

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big(b_{s,t}^{ij}+b_{s,t}^{kj}\Big),\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big(b_{s,t}^{li}+b_{s,t}^{lk}\Big)\bigg),

has the same distribution as
\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,b_{s,t}^{li}\big)
and hence will have the same fourth moments,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big(b_{s,t}^{ij}+b_{s,t}^{kj}\Big)+\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\Big(b_{s,t}^{li}+b_{s,t}^{lk}\Big)\bigg)^{4}\,\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}+b_{s,t}^{li}\big)^{4}\Big].

Since
b_{s,t}^{ij}+b_{s,t}^{li}
has the same distribution as
\sqrt{2}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{ij}
, the right-hand side equals
\frac{7}{60}h^{4}
. By reflecting the
k
-th coordinate of the
B
and using
\big(b_{s,t}^{kj},b_{s,t}^{lk}\big)=-\big(b_{s,t}^{jk},b_{s,t}^{kl}\big)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(b_{s,t}^{ij}+b_{s,t}^{jk}+b_{s,t}^{kl}+b_{s,t}^{li}\Big)^{4}\,\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{15}h^{4}.

(40)

By reflecting the
i
-th or
l
-th corrdinate of
B
, we see the following moments are zero.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{kl}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptb_{s,t}^{li}\Big]=0.

Several other terms in the expansion of (
40
) are computable using independence, e.g.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\big(b_{s,t}^{kl}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{kl}\big)^{2}\Big]=\frac{1}{144}h^{4}.

Therefore, expanding the left-hand side of (
40
) and using moments (
28
) and (
36
) gives,

\displaystyle 4\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]+12\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\big(b_{s,t}^{kl}\big)^{2}\Big]+24\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\big(b_{s,t}^{jk}\big)^{2}\Big]+24\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}b_{s,t}^{kl}b_{s,t}^{li}\Big]=\frac{7}{15}h^{4}

\displaystyle\implies\,\mathbb{E}\Big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}b_{s,t}^{kl}b_{s,t}^{li}\Big]=\frac{1}{24}\bigg(\frac{7}{15}-4\times\frac{7}{240}-12\times\frac{1}{144}-24\times\frac{7}{720}\bigg)h^{4}=\frac{1}{720}h^{4},

as required.
∎

Finally, we will consider the remaining moments of the Brownian bridge Lévy area. Using the symmetry of
B
, it will be straightforward to show these moments are zero.

Theorem 8

(Zero moments of
b_{s,t}
)
.

Apart from the moments in Theorems
5
,
6
,
7
and Remark
4
, all other degree
k
moments of the pair
(H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,b_{s,t})
with
k\leq 5
will be zero.

Proof.

We will first consider the following moment,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i_{1}}\cdots H_{s,t}^{i_{m}}\big)\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{2}}\cdots b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{n-1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{n}}\big)\Big],

(41)

where
I=\big(i_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,i_{m}\big)
and
J=\big(j_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,j_{n}\big)
are multi-indices with
i_{k}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{k}\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
. For each index
k\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
, we can count its number of appearances in
I
and
J
as

\displaystyle N_{k}:=|\hskip 0.7113ptp:i_{p}=k,i_{p}\in I\hskip 0.7113pt|+|\hskip 0.7113ptq:j_{q}=k,j_{q}\in J\hskip 0.7113pt|.

We will first consider the case when there exists
k\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
such that
N_{k}
is odd.
Letting
k
be fixed, we define a new Brownian bridge
\overline{B}
by flipping the
k
-th coordinate,

\displaystyle\overline{B}^{\hskip 0.7113pti}:=\begin{cases}B^{i},&\text{ if }i\neq k,\\
-B^{i},&\text{ if }i=k.\end{cases}

Similarly, we define the associated space-time Lévy area and space-space Lévy area,

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113pti}:=\begin{cases}H_{s,t}^{i},&\text{ if }i\neq k,\\[2.0pt]
-H_{s,t}^{i},&\text{ if }i=k,\end{cases}\hskip 28.45274pt\overline{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=\begin{cases}b_{s,t}^{ij},&\text{ if }k\notin\{i,j\}\text{ or }i=j,\\[2.0pt]
-b_{s,t}^{ij},&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

As
B
and
\overline{B}
have the same distribution, we see that the following moments are equal,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i_{1}}\cdots H_{s,t}^{i_{m}}\big)\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{2}}\cdots b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{n-1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{n}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overline{H}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113pti_{1}}\cdots\overline{H}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113pti_{m}}\big)\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overline{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{2}}\cdots\overline{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{n-1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{n}}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{N_{k}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i_{1}}\cdots H_{s,t}^{i_{m}}\big)\big(b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{2}}\cdots b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptj_{n-1}\hskip 0.7113pt,j_{n}}\big)\Big].

Since we assume
N_{k}
is odd, it immediately follows that the above expectation is zero.
Finally, we shall consider the case where
N_{k}
is even for all indices
k\in\{1,\cdots,d\}
. Using that each coordinate of
B
is independent and identically distributed, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{jk}b_{s,t}^{ki}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{kj}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptji}b_{s,t}^{ik}\big]

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{ki}\big],

where the second lines follows by the antisymmetry of Lévy area, i.e.
b_{s,t}^{ij}=-b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptji}
.
Hence
\,\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{jk}b_{s,t}^{ki}\big]=0
. Using the conditional expectation in Theorem
6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}b_{s,t}^{ij}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}b_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]\big]

\displaystyle=0.

The remaining moments (with
N_{k}
even) have been shown in Theorems
5
,
6
,
7
or can simply be computed using independence of the coordinates in the Brownian bridge.
∎

Remark 7
.

Using Theorems
5
,
7
and
8
, it is straightforward to compute any moment (with degree at most five) of Brownian Lévy area conditional on the increment
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
. To the author’s knowledge, such conditional moments of
A
were unknown prior to
Foster (2020)
.

3.3
Moment-matching approximation of Brownian Lévy area

In this section, we present the weak approximation
(
Foster, 2020
, Definition 7.3.5)
which we will show matches the majority of conditional moments established in sections
3.1
and
3.2
.

Definition 7

(Weak approximation of Brownian Lévy area)
.

Using the increment
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, space-time Lévy area
H_{s,t}
and space-time-time Lévy area
K_{s,t}
on
[s,t]
, we define

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(42)

where, conditional on
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, the
d\times d
random matrix
\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
has entries given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=\begin{cases}\sigma_{s,t}^{ij}\,\xi_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,&\text{ if }\,\,i<j,\\[3.0pt]
-\sigma_{s,t}^{ji}\,\xi_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptji}\hskip 0.7113pt,&\text{ if }\,\,i>j,\\[3.0pt]
0,&\text{ if }\,\,i=j,\end{cases}

for
1\leq i,j\leq d
with the independent random variables
\sigma_{s,t}^{ij}
and
\xi_{s,t}^{ij}
defined for
i<j
as

\displaystyle\xi_{s,t}^{ij}

\displaystyle\sim\begin{cases}\mathrm{Uniform}\big[-\sqrt{3}\hskip 0.7113pt,\sqrt{3}\big],&\text{with probability }\,p,\\[3.0pt]
\mathrm{Rademacher},&\text{with probability }\,1-p,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\sigma_{s,t}^{ij}

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{3}{28}\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big)\big(C^{j}+c\big)h^{2}+\frac{1}{28}h\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)},

where
c,p
are constants and
\{C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}\}
are independent exponential random variables with

\displaystyle C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}

\displaystyle\sim\mathrm{Exponential}\Big(\frac{15}{8}\Big),

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sqrt{3}}{3}-\frac{8}{15}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle:=\frac{21130}{25621}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Theorem 9
.

Let
\widetilde{A}_{s,t}
be the weak approximation of
A_{s,t}
given by Definition
7
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{A}_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{n_{1}}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{n_{1}}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\Big],

(43)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{A}_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{n_{2}}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{n_{2}}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big],

(44)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{A}_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{n_{3}}\big(\widetilde{A}_{s,t}^{jk}\big)^{n_{4}}\big(\widetilde{A}_{s,t}^{ki}\big)^{n_{5}}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{n_{3}}\big(A_{s,t}^{jk}\big)^{n_{4}}\big(A_{s,t}^{ki}\big)^{n_{5}}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\Big],

(45)

for distinct
i,j,k\in\{1,\cdots\hskip-0.7113pt,d\}
where
n_{m}\geq 0
with
n_{1}\leq 3
,
n_{2}\leq 5
and
n_{3}+n_{4}+n_{5}\leq 5
.

Proof.

Combining equations (
26
) and (
33
), gives the following decomposition of
A_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
,

\displaystyle A_{s,t}^{ij}=H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle+12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)

(46)

\displaystyle+720\big(M_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}-K_{s,t}^{i}M_{s,t}^{j}\big)+c_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

where
M_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{100800}hI_{d}\big)
and the Lévy area
c_{s,t}
are defined by equations (
34
)

and (
35
) respectively. In particular,
(M_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,c_{s,t})
and
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t})
are independent. Since equation (
46
) can be further extended to a polynomial expansion of Lévy area
Foster and
Habermann (2023)
,
by using the same techniques as in the proof of Theorem
4
, we obtain the moments

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[A_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{28}h^{2}+\frac{36}{7}h\Big(\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Skew}\big(A_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=0,

which are clearly matched by the weak approximation
\widetilde{A}_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
(i.e. equation (
43
) holds).
Moreover, applying the tower law shows the corresponding expectations in equations (
43
) and (
44
) are also matched. We now consider the following fourth moment of
\widetilde{a}_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\,\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\sigma_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\xi_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{9}{5}\cdot p+1\cdot(1-p)\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\frac{3}{28}\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big)\big(C^{j}+c\big)h^{2}+\frac{1}{28}h\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big)^{2}\,\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{42525}{25621}\Bigg(\bigg(\frac{3}{28}\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big)\big(C^{j}+c\big)h^{2}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{1}{28}h\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 49.79231pt+2\bigg(\frac{3}{28}\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big)\big(C^{j}+c\big)h^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{28}h\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{42525}{25621}\Bigg(\frac{9}{784}\bigg(\Big(\frac{8}{15}\Big)^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\bigg)^{2}+2\times\frac{3}{28}\times\frac{1}{3}\times\frac{1}{28}\times\frac{2}{5}+\frac{1}{784}\times\frac{1}{25}\times 8\Bigg)h^{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{27}{2800}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}.

We also note that
\widetilde{a}_{s,t}
is independent of
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t})
and correctly correlated with
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{n}\big(\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{m}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{n}\big(\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{m}\,\big|\,K_{s,t}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{n}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{m}\,\big|\,K_{s,t}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{n}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{m}\,\big|\,K_{s,t}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{n}\big(a_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{m}\,\big|\,K_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{n}\big(a_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{m}\Big],

for
n\geq 0
and
m\in\{0,1,2,3\}
. Since these moments coincide for
\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}
and
a_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{4}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+a_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{4}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Big],

which gives equation (
44
) for
n_{2}=4
. The moments also match when
n_{2}=5
since the symmetry of
(K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{a}_{s,t})
and
(K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,a_{s,t})
means that all of the odd moments are zero.
We now consider some cross-moments of the approximation which appear when
d\geq 3
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{2}\big(\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\frac{3}{28}\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big)\big(C^{j}+c\big)h^{2}+\frac{1}{28}h\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt\Big(\frac{3}{28}\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113ptj}+c\big)\big(C^{k}+c\big)h^{2}+\frac{1}{28}h\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{k}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{3}{28}\Big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113ptj}+c\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\big[C^{\hskip 0.7113ptk}+c\big]h^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{6}{28^{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\big(C^{\hskip 0.7113pti}+c\big)\big(C^{j}+c\big)\big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{k}\big)^{2}\Big]h^{3}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{28^{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big(\big(12K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}+\big(12K_{s,t}^{k}\big)^{2}\Big)\Big]h^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{3}{28^{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{3}+\Big(\frac{8}{15}\Big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big)+\frac{2}{28^{2}}\times\frac{2}{5}+\frac{1}{28^{2}}\times\frac{6}{25}\bigg)h^{4}=\frac{31}{8400}h^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Letting
\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}
be the approximate bridge area, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{2}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)\big)^{2}\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{k}-H_{s,t}^{j}K_{s,t}^{k}\big)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{2}\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{k}-H_{s,t}^{j}K_{s,t}^{k}\big)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)\big)^{2}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{2}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{144}\times\frac{1}{25}\Big)\Big(1+3+3+1\Big)h^{4}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{2}\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{k}\big)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(12\big(H_{s,t}^{j}K_{s,t}^{k}\big)\big)^{2}\Big]+\frac{31}{8400}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{450}+24\bigg(\frac{1}{720}\times\frac{1}{28}+\frac{3}{28}\times\frac{144}{720^{2}}+\frac{1}{28}\times\frac{144}{720^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 38.41121pt+2\hskip 0.7113pt\Big(\frac{1}{28}+\frac{1}{28}\times\frac{2}{5}\Big)\times\frac{1}{5}\times\frac{1}{12}+\frac{31}{8400}\bigg)h^{4}=\frac{7}{720}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\big)\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{k}-H_{s,t}^{j}K_{s,t}^{k}\big)+\widetilde{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{k}\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)\big)\big(12\big(K_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{k}-H_{s,t}^{j}K_{s,t}^{k}\big)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=-144\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{k}\big)^{2}\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]=-\frac{1}{720}\hskip 0.7113pth^{3}.

By the independence of
\xi_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, it follows that the odd moments of
\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{a}_{s,t}\big)
are zero – and hence match the moments of
\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,a_{s,t}\big)
. Therefore, since
\widetilde{b}_{s,t}
has the same fourth moments as
b_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, the conditional expectations (
45
) also match.
∎

Remark 8
.

The proposed weak approximation does not match all fourth moments

since
\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}b_{s,t}^{kl}b_{s,t}^{li}\big]=\frac{1}{720}h^{4}
and
\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\,\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{kl}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{li}\big]=2\times\frac{1}{144}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\times\frac{1}{25}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}=\frac{1}{1800}h^{4}
.

From the experiments in section
5.2
, we see that the weak approximation given by Definition
7
can outperform standard and recent Lévy area approximations
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
;
Davie (2014)
;
Mrongowius and
Rößler (2022)
.

However, due to Remark
8
, the approximation will not match the fourth moment
\mathbb{E}\big[A_{s,t}^{ij}A_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}A_{s,t}^{kl}A_{s,t}^{li}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\,\big]
. In an effort to reduce this mismatch for the case when
d=4
, we consider replacing
\xi_{s,t}
with a random matrix
\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}
such that
\mathbb{E}\big[\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptkl}\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\,li}\big]>0
.

Definition 8

(Subtly correlated random matrix)
.

Let
Z^{1,2}
,
Z^{1,3}
,
Z^{1,4}
and
Z^{2,3}
denote independent Rademacher random variables and suppose
U:=(U_{1},U_{2})\sim\mathrm{Uniform}\big(\{(1,1),(1,-1),(-1,-1)\}\big)
. We define random variables
Z^{2,4}
and
Z^{3,4}
as

\displaystyle Z^{2,4}

\displaystyle:=Z^{2,3}Z^{1,3}Z^{1,4}U_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 14.22636ptZ^{3,4}:=Z^{2,3}Z^{1,2}Z^{1,4}U_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Then, using
\xi_{s,t}
from definition
7
, we define a “subtly correlated” random matrix as

\displaystyle\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=\begin{cases}|\hskip 0.7113pt\xi_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt|Z^{ij},&\text{if }i<j,\\[2.0pt]
-|\hskip 0.7113pt\xi_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt|Z^{ij},&\text{if }i>j,\\[1.0pt]
\,0,&\text{if }i=j.\end{cases}

Theorem 10
.

The matrices
\xi_{s,t}
and
\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}
have the same moments up to degree 5 except

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptkl}\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptli}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{3841489152965701849}{20683572037203882288}+\frac{29202537105993615}{430907750775080881}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{3}\approx 0.30\,\,\,(\text{2.d.p})\hskip 0.7113pt,

for distinct
i,j,k,l\in\{1,2,3,4\}
where we define
Z^{ji}:=-Z^{ij}
for
i>j
.

Proof.

By the definitions of
Z^{2,4}
and
Z^{3,4}
, the following fourth moments are non-zero,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{1,2}Z^{2,3}Z^{3,4}Z^{4,1}\big]

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{1,2}Z^{2,3}\big(Z^{2,3}Z^{1,2}Z^{1,4}U_{2}\big)Z^{1,4}\big]=-\mathbb{E}\big[U_{2}\big]=\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{1,4}Z^{4,2}Z^{2,3}Z^{3,1}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{1,4}\big(Z^{2,3}Z^{1,3}Z^{1,4}U_{1}\big)Z^{2,3}Z^{1,3}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[U_{1}\big]=\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{1,3}Z^{3,4}Z^{4,2}Z^{2,1}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{1,3}\big(Z^{2,3}Z^{1,2}Z^{1,4}U_{2}\big)\big(Z^{2,3}Z^{1,3}Z^{1,4}U_{1}\big)Z^{1,2}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[U_{1}U_{2}\big]=\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113pt.

On the other hand, it can be verified by direct calculation that the remaining fourth moments of
Z
will be zero. This can be shown as, even after simplifying the products, there will still be at least one independent Rademacher random variable remaining.
Similarly, it is straightforward to show that the other moments
\xi_{s,t}
and
\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}
match.
Finally, by the independence of
\{\xi_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,\xi_{s,t}^{jk}\hskip 0.7113pt,\xi_{s,t}^{kl}\hskip 0.7113pt,\xi_{s,t}^{li}\}
and the value
p=\frac{21130}{25621}\hskip 0.7113pt
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\hskip 0.7113pt\xi_{s,t}^{ij}||\hskip 0.7113pt\xi_{s,t}^{jk}||\hskip 0.7113pt\xi_{s,t}^{kl}||\hskip 0.7113pt\xi_{s,t}^{li}|\big]

\displaystyle=\bigg(p+(1-p)\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg)^{4}=\bigg(\frac{21130}{25621}+\frac{4491}{51242}\,\sqrt{3}\bigg)^{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{3841489152965701849}{6894524012401294096}+\frac{87607611317980845}{430907750775080881}\,\sqrt{3}\hskip 0.7113pt.

The result now immediately follows by the independence of
\xi
and
Z
.
∎

Due to Theorem
10
, we would expect that using
\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}
instead of
\xi_{s,t}
in Definition
7
(when
d=4
), should result in an improved weak approximation
\wideparen{A}_{s,t}
as it still satisfies Theorem
9
whilst better matching the non-trivial fourth moment
\mathbb{E}\big[A_{s,t}^{ij}A_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}A_{s,t}^{kl}A_{s,t}^{li}\,\big]
.

Definition 9

(Modified weak approximation of Lévy area)
.

Using the increment
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, space-time Lévy area
H_{s,t}
and space-time-time Lévy area
K_{s,t}
on
[s,t]
, we define

\displaystyle\wideparen{A}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+12\big(K_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-H_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\wideparen{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(47)

where, conditional on
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, the
d\times d
random matrix
\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{a}_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
has entries given by

\displaystyle\wideparen{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}:=\begin{cases}\sigma_{s,t}^{ij}\,\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt,&\text{ if }\,\,i>j,\\[3.0pt]
-\sigma_{s,t}^{ij}\,\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptji}\hskip 0.7113pt,&\text{ if }\,\,i<j,\\[3.0pt]
0,&\text{ if }\,\,i=j,\end{cases}

for
1\leq i,j\leq d
where
\sigma_{s,t}
was given in Definition
7
and
\wideparen{\xi}_{s,t}
was given in Definition
8
.

Remark 9
.

By applying either Monte Carlo simulation or Gaussian quadrature to estimate
\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma_{0,1}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma_{0,1}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma_{0,1}^{\hskip 0.7113ptkl}\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma_{0,1}^{\hskip 0.7113ptli}\big]
, we observe that the associated fourth moment of
\wideparen{a}_{s,t}
is

\displaystyle\,\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,\wideparen{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptkl}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{a}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptli}\big]\approx 0.00074\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}\,\,(\text{2.s.f}\hskip 0.7113pt),

which would imply that

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\mathbb{E}\big[\,\wideparen{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptij}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptkl}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptli}\big]\approx 0.0013\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}\,\,(\text{2.s.f}\hskip 0.7113pt),\hskip 113.81102pt

where
\wideparen{b}_{s,t}:=12\big(K_{s,t}\otimes H_{s,t}-H_{s,t}\otimes K_{s,t}\big)+\wideparen{a}_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
denotes the weak approximation of the Brownian bridge Lévy area. Note that this is substantially closer to the true value of

\mathbb{E}\big[b_{s,t}^{ij}b_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}b_{s,t}^{kl}b_{s,t}^{li}\big]=\frac{1}{720}h^{4}\approx 0.0014\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}
than
\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\,\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptjk}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{kl}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{b}_{s,t}^{li}\big]=\frac{1}{1800}h^{4}\approx 0.00056\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}
.

However, it is unclear how to extend the new approximation (
47
) to higher dimensions.

4
Space-space-time Lévy area of Brownian motion

In this section, we will consider approximations for the “space-space-time” Lévy area.
This will be relevant when designing high order numerical methods for SDEs satisfying the commutativity condition (
5
), which includes both additive and scalar noise types.
We note that the main results of the section (Theorems
14
and
15
) are new, but already known when
d=1
(see
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Foster (2020)
). We first introduce some useful notation for integrals.

4.1
The shuffle product for calculations with iterated integrals

Definition 10
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
denote the set of words with letters from
\mathcal{A}_{d}:=\{0,1,\cdots,d\}
. Let
\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
be the space of non-commutative polynomials in
\mathcal{A}_{d}
with real coefficients. We can identify linear combinations of integrals with elements in
\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
by
I_{e}:=1
and

\displaystyle i_{1}\cdots\hskip 0.7113pti_{m}

\displaystyle\longleftrightarrow I_{i_{1}\cdots\hskip 0.7113pti_{m}}:=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{r_{1}}\dots\int_{s}^{r_{m-1}}\circ\,dW^{i_{1}}_{r_{n}}\circ dW^{i_{2}}_{r_{m-1}}\cdots\circ dW^{i_{m-1}}_{r_{2}}\circ dW^{i_{m}}_{r_{1}}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\lambda u+\mu v

\displaystyle\longleftrightarrow I_{\lambda u+\mu v}:=\lambda I_{u}+\mu I_{v}\hskip 0.7113pt,

for
m\geq 0
,
i_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,i_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt,\cdots,i_{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{d}\hskip 0.7113pt
,
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{d}^{\ast}
and
\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt,\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, where we define
W_{t}^{0}:=t\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Using the above notation, we can express integration by parts as a “shuffle” project.

Definition 11
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}_{d}
is a set containing
d
letters and let
\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
be the corresponding space of non-commutative polynomials in
\mathcal{A}_{d}
with real coefficients.
Then the shuffle product
\,\sqcup\mathchoice{\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern-1.0mu}\sqcup:\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle\times\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
is the unique bilinear map such that

\displaystyle ua\sqcup\mathchoice{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}\sqcup\hskip 2.13394pt\,vb

\displaystyle=(u\sqcup\mathchoice{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}\sqcup\hskip 2.13394ptvb)\hskip 0.7113pta+(ua\sqcup\mathchoice{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}\sqcup\hskip 2.13394ptv)\hskip 0.7113ptb,

\displaystyle u\sqcup\mathchoice{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}\sqcup\hskip 2.13394pte

\displaystyle=e\sqcup\mathchoice{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-7.0mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}{\mkern-3.5mu}\sqcup\hskip 2.13394ptu=u,

where
e
denotes the empty letter.

This shuffle project will become useful for expanding products of iterated integrals.

Theorem 11

(Integration by parts formula)
.

For all
\hskip 0.7113ptu,v\in\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
, we have that

\displaystyle I_{u}\cdot I_{v}=I_{u\hskip 0.7113pt\sqcup\hskip-1.5649pt\sqcup\hskip 0.7113ptv}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(48)

Proof.

It is clear that the identity (
48
) holds when
u=e
or
v=e
since
I_{e}=1
.
Suppose that (
48
) holds for all words
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{d}^{\ast}
with a combined length less than
m
. Then for words
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{d}^{\ast}\hskip 0.7113pt
and letters
a,b\in\mathcal{A}_{d}\hskip 0.7113pt
such that
|ua|+|vb|=m
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}I_{u}(r)\circ dW_{r}^{a}\int_{s}^{t}I_{v}(r)\circ dW_{r}^{b}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\bigg(\int_{s}^{r_{1}}I_{u}(r_{2})\circ dW_{r_{2}}^{a}\bigg)\circ d\bigg(\int_{s}^{r_{1}}I_{v}(r_{2})\circ dW_{r_{2}}^{b}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\int_{s}^{t}\bigg(\int_{s}^{r_{1}}I_{v}(r_{2})\circ dW_{r_{2}}^{b}\bigg)\circ d\bigg(\int_{s}^{r_{1}}I_{u}(r_{2})\circ dW_{r_{2}}^{a}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}I_{ua}(r_{1})\hskip 0.7113ptI_{v}(r_{1})\circ dW_{r_{1}}^{b}+\int_{s}^{t}I_{vb}(r_{1})\hskip 0.7113ptI_{u}(r_{1})\circ dW_{r_{1}}^{a},

where the second line uses integration by parts (which holds for Stratonovich integrals).
Since
ua
and
vb
have a combined length of
m
, applying the induction hypothesis gives

\displaystyle I_{ua}\cdot I_{vb}

\displaystyle=I_{(ua\hskip 0.7113pt\sqcup\hskip-1.5649pt\sqcup\hskip 0.7113ptv)\hskip 0.7113ptb}+I_{(u\hskip 0.7113pt\sqcup\hskip-1.5649pt\sqcup\hskip 0.7113ptvb)\hskip 0.7113pta}=I_{(ua\hskip 0.7113pt\sqcup\hskip-1.5649pt\sqcup\hskip 0.7113ptv)\hskip 0.7113ptb+(u\hskip 0.7113pt\sqcup\hskip-1.5649pt\sqcup\hskip 0.7113ptvb)\hskip 0.7113pta}=I_{ua\hskip 0.7113pt\sqcup\hskip-1.5649pt\sqcup\hskip 0.7113ptvb}\hskip 0.7113pt,

which is an immediate consequence of the shuffle product’s recursive definition.
∎

Using Theorem
11
, it will be straightforward to rewrite products of integrals as linear combinations of (high order) integrals. In addition, it can be used to establish decompositions of iterated integrals into symmetric and antisymmetric components.

Theorem 12

(Decomposition of integrals into symmetric and antisymmetric parts)
.

Let the Lie bracket
\hskip 0.7113pt[\,\cdot\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113pt\cdot\hskip 0.7113pt]:\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle\times\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\langle\mathcal{A}_{d}\rangle
be the unique bilinear map with

\displaystyle[u,v]=uv-vu\hskip 0.7113pt,

(49)

for words
u,v\in\mathcal{A}_{d}^{\ast}\hskip 0.7113pt
. Then, adopting the notation of Definition
10
and Theorem
11
