, we have

\displaystyle I_{ij}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}I_{i}\cdot I_{j}+\frac{1}{2}I_{[i,j]}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(50)

\displaystyle I_{ijk}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}I_{i}\cdot I_{j}\cdot I_{k}+\frac{1}{4}I_{i}\cdot I_{[j,k]}+\frac{1}{4}I_{[i,j]}\cdot I_{k}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[[i,j],k]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[i,[j,k]]}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(51)

for
i,j,k\in\mathcal{A}_{d}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Proof.

The results follow by expanding the Lie brackets
[\,\cdot\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113pt\cdot\hskip 0.7113pt]
using (
49
) and applying the shuffle product (which, by Theorem
11
, is simply performing integration by parts).

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\frac{1}{6}I_{i}\cdot I_{j}\cdot I_{k}+\frac{1}{4}I_{i}\cdot I_{[j,k]}+\frac{1}{4}I_{[i,j]}\cdot I_{k}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[[i,j],k]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[i,[j,k]]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}(I_{ij}+I_{ji})\cdot I_{k}+\frac{1}{4}I_{i}\cdot I_{jk}-\frac{1}{4}I_{i}\cdot I_{kj}+\frac{1}{4}I_{ij}\cdot I_{k}-\frac{1}{4}I_{ji}\cdot I_{k}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{6}I_{[ij,k]}-\frac{1}{6}I_{[ji,k]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[i,jk]}-\frac{1}{6}I_{[i,kj]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}(I_{ijk}+I_{ikj}+I_{kij}+I_{jik}+I_{jki}+I_{kji})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{4}(I_{ijk}+I_{jik}+I_{jki})-\frac{1}{4}(I_{ikj}+I_{kij}+I_{kji})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{4}(I_{ijk}+I_{ikj}+I_{kij})-\frac{1}{4}(I_{jik}+I_{jki}+I_{kji})

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{6}(I_{ijk}-I_{jik}-I_{jik}+I_{kji})+\frac{1}{6}(I_{ijk}-I_{jki}-I_{ikj}+I_{kji})

\displaystyle=I_{ijk}\hskip 0.7113pt,

and just as for Theorem
3
, we have
I_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}(I_{ij}+I_{ji})+\frac{1}{2}(I_{ij}-I_{ji})=\frac{1}{2}I_{i}\cdot I_{j}+\frac{1}{2}I_{[i,j]}\hskip 0.7113pt
.
∎

4.2
Approximations of space-space-time Lévy area

In practice, we are usually interested in approximating these third iterated integrals when
k=0
and the diffusion vector fields
\{g_{i}\}
satisfy the commutativity condition:

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912ptg_{i}^{\prime}(y)g_{j}(y)=g_{j}^{\prime}(y)g_{i}(y),\hskip 28.45274pt\forall y\in\mathbb{R}^{e}.

(52)

As discussed in Remark
2
, this simplifies the SDE’s Taylor expansion and means that (space-space) Lévy area is not needed. However, this
(i,j)
-commutativity also extends to the third iterated integrals and therefore we only need to approximate the sums,

\displaystyle I_{ij0}+I_{ji0}\hskip 0.7113pt,\quad I_{i0j}+I_{j0i}\hskip 0.7113pt,\quad I_{0ij}+I_{0ji}\hskip 0.7113pt.

This leads us to the following definition for the “space-space-time” Lévy area of
W
.

Definition 12

(Space-space-time Lévy area)
.

Over an interval
[s,t]
, we will define the (space-space-time) Lévy area of Brownian motion as a
d\times d
matrix
L_{s,t}
with entries

\displaystyle L_{s,t}^{i,j}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{12}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}^{i}\circ dW_{v}^{j}\,du+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}^{j}\circ dW_{v}^{i}\,du

(53)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-2\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}^{i}\,dv\circ dW_{u}^{j}-2\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}^{j}\,dv\circ dW_{u}^{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}(v-s)\,dW_{v}^{i}\circ dW_{u}^{j}+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}(v-s)\,dW_{v}^{j}\circ dW_{u}^{i}\bigg),

Remark 10
.

Using the notation from section
4.1
, we have
L_{s,t}^{i,j}=\frac{1}{12}\big(I_{[i,[j,0]]}+I_{[j,[i,0]]}\big)
.

Similar to Theorem
2
, we can reconstruct iterated integrals using
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,L_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Theorem 13

(Relationship between space-space-time Lévy area and other integrals)
.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(I_{ij0}+I_{ji0}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}hW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{4}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)+L_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(I_{i0j}+I_{j0i}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}hW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-2L_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big(I_{0ij}+I_{0ji}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}hW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{4}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)+L_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

By Theorem
12
, we have

\displaystyle I_{ij0}+I_{ji0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}I_{i}I_{j}h+\frac{1}{4}I_{i}\cdot I_{[j,0]}+\frac{1}{4}I_{j}\cdot I_{[i,0]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[i,[j,0]]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[j,[i,0]]}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle I_{i0j}+I_{j0i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}I_{i}I_{j}h+\frac{1}{3}I_{[[i,0],j]}+\frac{1}{3}I_{[i,[0,j]]}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle I_{0ij}+I_{0ji}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}I_{i}\cdot I_{j}h-\frac{1}{4}I_{i}\cdot I_{[j,0]}-\frac{1}{4}I_{j}\cdot I_{[i,0]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[[0,i],j]}+\frac{1}{6}I_{[[0,j],i]}\hskip 0.7113pt.

The result now follows as
hH_{s,t}^{i}=\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113ptdu-\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,t}^{i}=\frac{1}{2}I_{[i,0]}
and

\hskip 21.33955ptL_{s,t}^{ij}=\frac{1}{12}\big(I_{[i,[j,0]]}+I_{[j,[i,0]]}\big)=-\frac{1}{12}\big(I_{[i,[0,j]]}+I_{[[i,0],j]}\big)=\frac{1}{12}\big(I_{[[0,j],i]}+I_{[[0,i],j]}\big).

∎

Corollary 1
.

Given
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,L_{s,t}
, we can recover the following Brownian integral,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdu=\frac{1}{3}hW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)+2L_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(54)

Proof.

Using integration by parts, we have
W_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}^{i}\circ dW_{v}^{j}+\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,v}^{j}\circ dW_{v}^{i}
. Thus, the left-hand side is equal to
I_{ij0}+I_{ji0}
and the result follows by Theorem
13
.
∎

Using equation (
54
), we shall derive our first approximation of the space-space-time Lévy area
L_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, which will simply be its expectation conditional on
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t})
.

Theorem 14

(Approximation of space-space-time Lévy area)
.

For
\hskip 0.7113pt0\leq s\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]=\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{30}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(55)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\big]=\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+K_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)

(56)

\displaystyle+\frac{60}{7}hK_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{3}{140}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij},

where
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta.

Proof.

By the polynomial expansion of Brownian motion (see
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Habermann (2021)
), we have that

\displaystyle W_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{W}_{t}+\widetilde{Z}_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(57)

where
\widetilde{W}=\{\widetilde{W}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
denotes the cubic polynomial approximation of
W
given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{t}

\displaystyle:=tW_{1}+6t(1-t)H_{0,1}+60t(1-t)(1-2t)K_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(58)

and
\widetilde{Z}=\{\widetilde{Z}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
is a centred Gaussian process and independent of
W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}
.
With the above decomposition of Brownian motion, we can compute the expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}W_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\big(\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}+\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\big)\big(\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}+\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\big)dt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\,\big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\big]dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt+\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\,\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\,\big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\big]dt+\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\,Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}\,\big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\big]dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\big]dt+\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\big]dt+\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\big]dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\big]dt.

Since the first term is the integral of a polynomial, it can be explicitly computed as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=W_{1}\int_{0}^{1}t(tW_{1}^{j}+6t(1-t)H_{0,1}^{j}+60t(1-t)(1-2t)K_{0,1}^{j})dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt+H_{0,1}\int_{0}^{1}6t(1-t)(tW_{1}^{j}+6t(1-t)H_{0,1}^{j}+60t(1-t)(1-2t)K_{0,1}^{j})dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt+K_{0,1}\int_{0}^{1}60t(1-t)(1-2t)(tW_{1}^{j}+6t(1-t)H_{0,1}^{j}+60t(1-t)(1-2t)K_{0,1}^{j})dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{3}hW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+H_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\frac{6}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}+K_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 78.52962pt+\frac{120}{7}hK_{s,t}^{i}K_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Hence,
\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\big]dt=\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}W_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}dt\big]=\frac{1}{2}\delta_{ij}-\frac{16}{35}\delta_{ij}=\frac{3}{70}\delta_{ij}
.

The second result (
56
) now follows by Corollary
1
and the standard Brownian scaling. By taking the expectation of this conditional on
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t})
, we then obtain (
55
).
∎

Remark 11
.

By rearranging the terms in equation (
54
), it is possible to show that

\displaystyle L_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}\bigg(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\bigg)^{\otimes 2}du-\frac{1}{2}h\big(W_{s,t}\otimes K_{s,t}+K_{s,t}\otimes W_{s,t}\big).

So by the independence of
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t})
, taking the conditional expectation gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\bigg(W_{s,u}-\frac{u-s}{h}W_{s,t}\bigg)^{\otimes 2}du\,\Big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\Bigg].

Therefore, in the typical setting where the SDE solver only requires
W_{s,t}
or
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t})
we can interpret
L_{s,t}
as measuring the discrepancy between the Brownian motion and its straight line approximation. However, this interpretation changes when
K_{s,t}
is used.

Since the approximations given by the right-hand sides of equations (
55
) and (
56
) are conditional expectations of
L_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, they will respectively produce the least
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
error among the
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t})
-measurable and
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t})
-measurable estimators.
Hence, for completeness, we will also compute explicit formulae for these
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
errors.

Theorem 15

(Mean-squared error of space-space-time Lévy area approximations)
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(\hskip 0.7113ptL_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=V_{s,t}^{ij}+\frac{1}{1440}h^{3}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

(59)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{1400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(\hskip 0.7113ptL_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{V}_{s,t}^{ij}+\frac{1}{1400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

(60)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{98}h^{3}\Big(\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(K_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big),

where

\displaystyle V_{s,t}^{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{11}{25200}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i=j,\\
\frac{1}{900}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i\neq j,\end{cases}\hskip 28.45274pt\widetilde{V}_{s,t}^{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{11}{88200}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i=j,\\
\frac{9}{19600}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i\neq j.\end{cases}

Proof.

As in previous proofs, we will use the stochastic integral given by equation (
54
).
For simplicity, we will consider this integral on
[0,1]
and use the below decomposition,

\displaystyle W_{t}

\displaystyle=\wideparen{W}_{t}+Z_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

where
\wideparen{W}=\{\wideparen{W}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
is the quadratic polynomial approximation of
W
given by

\displaystyle\wideparen{W}_{t}

\displaystyle:=tW_{1}+6t(1-t)H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,

and
Z=\{Z_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
is a centred Gaussian process and independent of
W_{1}
and
H_{0,1}
.
With this decomposition, it is now possible to expand the conditional variance of (
54
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}W_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{t}^{j}dt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)

(61)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{t}^{j}dt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{t}^{j}dt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{t}^{j}dt\bigg)\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt+\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\bigg)\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt\bigg),

where the last line follows as
\wideparen{W}
is determined by
(W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1})
and
Z
is an independent Gaussian process which is symmetric (that is,
Z
and
-Z
have the same distribution).
We will now assume
i\neq j
and explicitly compute the different variances given above.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt\bigg)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(Z_{v}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]\,du\,dv.

Therefore, since the covariance function of each Brownian arch process is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s}^{i}Z_{t}^{i}\big]

\displaystyle=\min(s,t)-st-3st(1-s)(1-t),

see
(
Foster et al., 2024
, Definition 3.4)
, it directly follows from the independence of
Z^{i}
and
Z^{j}
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}Z_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}dt\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{v}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]\,du\,dv=\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\,du\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}u-u^{2}-3u^{2}(1-u)^{2}\,du\Bigg)^{2}=\bigg(\frac{1}{15}\bigg)^{2}=\frac{1}{225}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Similarly, we can compute the conditional variances for the “cross-integrals” as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\bigg)=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\bigg)^{2}\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{v}^{i}\,\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptZ_{v}^{j}\big]\hskip 0.7113ptdu\hskip 0.7113ptdv

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{v}^{i}\,\big(\min(u,v)-uv-3uv(1-u)(1-v)\big)\hskip 0.7113ptdu\hskip 0.7113ptdv

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\wideparen{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{W}_{v}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\min(u,v)\hskip 0.7113ptdu\hskip 0.7113ptdv-\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}u\wideparen{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdu\bigg)^{2}-3\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}u(1-u)\wideparen{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdu\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{2}{15}\big(W_{1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{13}{35}\big(H_{0,1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{13}{30}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{1}^{i}H_{0,1}^{i}-\Big(\frac{1}{3}W_{1}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}H_{0,1}^{i}\Big)^{2}-3\Big(\frac{1}{12}W_{1}^{i}+\frac{1}{5}H_{0,1}^{i}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{720}\big(W_{1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{700}\big(H_{0,1}^{i}\big)^{2}.

When
i=j
, the conditional variance given by (
59
) was shown in
(
Foster et al., 2024
, Theorem 3.10)
.
By the same arguments, we can compute the variance (
60
) with the cubic polynomial decomposition of Brownian motion,
W\hskip-1.28036pt=\widetilde{W}+\widetilde{Z}
, previously used to prove Theorem
14
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}W_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{t}^{j}dt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\bigg)+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}dt\bigg),

where we used the independence and symmetry of
\widetilde{Z}
(similar to the expansion (
61
)).
Similar to before, we note that the covariance function for the Gaussian process
\widetilde{Z}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{Z}_{s}^{i}\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\big]

\displaystyle=\min(s,t)-st-3st(1-s)(1-t)-5st(1-s)(1-t)(1-2s)(1-2t),

which follows from the covariance function of
W
and the equations (
57
) and (
58
).

Using the above covariance function for
\widetilde{Z}
, we can compute the remaining variances.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}dt\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]\,du\,dv=\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\,du\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\big(u-u^{2}-3u^{2}(1-u)^{2}-5u^{2}(1-u)^{2}(1-2u)^{2}\big)\,du\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{3}{70}\bigg)^{2}=\frac{9}{4900}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\big(\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\big)^{2}\,dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\,du\,dv-\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\big(\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{i}\big)^{2}\,dt\Bigg]\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\hskip-1.42262pt\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\hskip-0.7113pt+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{i}\big]^{2}\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptdu\,dv-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\hskip 0.7113ptdu\,dv

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt=2\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{i}\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{i}\big]^{2}\,du\,dv=\frac{11}{22050}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\bigg)=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdt\bigg)^{2}\,\Big|\,W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{0,1}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{0,1}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{v}^{i}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{Z}_{u}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{Z}_{v}^{j}\big]\hskip 0.7113ptdu\hskip 0.7113ptdv

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{W}_{v}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\min(u,v)\hskip 0.7113ptdu\hskip 0.7113ptdv

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt-\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}u\widetilde{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdu\bigg)^{2}\hskip-1.42262pt-3\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}u(1-u)\widetilde{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdu\bigg)^{2}\hskip-1.42262pt-5\bigg(\int_{0}^{1}u(1-u)(1-2u)\widetilde{W}_{u}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdu\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{2}{15}\big(W_{1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{13}{35}\big(H_{0,1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{10}{7}\big(K_{0,1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{13}{30}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{1}^{i}H_{0,1}^{i}-H_{0,1}^{i}K_{0,1}^{i}-\frac{5}{7}W_{1}^{i}K_{0,1}^{i}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt-\Big(\frac{1}{3}W_{1}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}H_{0,1}^{i}-K_{0,1}^{i}\Big)^{2}-3\Big(\frac{1}{12}W_{1}^{i}+\frac{1}{5}H_{0,1}^{i}\Big)^{2}-5\Big(\frac{2}{7}K_{0,1}^{i}-\frac{1}{60}W_{0,1}^{i}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{700}\big(H_{0,1}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{49}\big(K_{0,1}^{i}\big)^{2}.

The variances for
L_{s,t}^{ij}
now follow by equation (
54
) and the usual Brownian scaling.
∎

Remark 12
.

Since the conditional variances (
59
) and (
60
) are simply the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
errors of the approximations given in Theorem
14
, they could also be used to estimate the
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
error produced by one step of a high order solver for a commutative noise SDE. These local error estimates would then be useful when adaptively reducing step sizes.
We refer the reader to
(
Foster, 2020
, Chapter 6)
for an example of such an adaptive methodology which exhibits high order convergence, even for a non-Lipschitz SDE (the CIR model).
However, the main downside of this approach is that it requires explicitly computing vector field derivatives – which can be particularly difficult for high dimensional SDEs.

4.3
Approximation of
L_{s,t}
for high order splitting methods

In this section, we will compute the mean and variance of
L_{s,t}
conditional on
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}
and a Rademacher random vector – which we expect is faster to generate than
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.
This approximation was proposed in
Foster et al. (2024)
for deriving high order splitting methods.
Similar to the previous section, our main theorem for approximating space-space-time Lévy area (Theorem
17
) is new but already known when
d=1
(see
(
Foster
et al., 2024
, Theorem B.4)
).
We first define the Rademacher random vector
n_{s,t}
that will be used instead of
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Definition 13
.

The space-time Lévy swing
1
1

1

The acronym “
swing
” stands for “
s
ide
w
ith
in
tegral
g
reater”.
of Brownian motion over
[s,t]
is given by

\displaystyle n_{s,t}:=\mathrm{sgn}\big(H_{s,u}-H_{u,t}\big),

(62)

where
u:=\frac{1}{2}(s+t)
is the interval’s midpoint.

Figure 5:
Space-time Lévy swing gives the side where the path has greater space-time Lévy area (diagram taken from
Foster (2020)
).

Just like
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, it is straightforward to show that
n_{s,t}
is independent of
\big(W_{s,t},H_{s,t}\big)
.
However, we will also establish the independence of the Brownian arch at
u=\frac{1}{2}(s+t)
, since it will become helpful for proving the main result of this section (Theorem
17
).

Theorem 16
.

Let
u=\frac{1}{2}(s+t)
be the midpoint of
[s,t]
. We define the random vectors,

\displaystyle Z_{s,u}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{8}\big(W_{s,u}-W_{u,t}\big)-\frac{3}{4}\big(H_{s,u}+H_{u,t}\big)\hskip 0.7113pt,

(63)

\displaystyle N_{s,t}

\displaystyle:=H_{s,u}-H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(64)

Then
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}
are independent and

\displaystyle Z_{s,u}\sim\mathcal{N}\bigg(0,\frac{1}{16}hI_{d}\bigg),\hskip 21.33955ptN_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\bigg(0,\frac{1}{12}hI_{d}\bigg).

Moreover, we can write
(W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t})
in terms of
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t})
as

\displaystyle W_{s,u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}+Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 14.22636pt\,\,\,W_{u,t}=\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}-\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}-Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(65)

\displaystyle H_{s,u}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}+\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 14.22636ptH_{u,t}=\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}-\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(66)

Proof.

Since
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}
and
N_{s,t}
can be expressed as linear functions of the same underlying Brownian motion
W
, it immediately follows that they are jointly normal. Thus, to show that they are independent, it suffices to show that they are uncorrelated.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[W_{s,t}\otimes Z_{s,u}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[W_{s,u}\otimes Z_{s,u}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[W_{u,t}\otimes Z_{s,u}\big]=\frac{1}{16}hI_{d}-\frac{1}{16}hI_{d}=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[W_{s,t}\otimes N_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[W_{s,u}\otimes(H_{s,u}-H_{u,t})\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[W_{u,t}\otimes(H_{s,u}-H_{u,t})\big]=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s,u}\otimes N_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[(H_{s,u}+H_{u,t})\otimes(H_{s,u}-H_{u,t})\big]=0.

To show that
H_{s,t}
is also uncorrelated with
Z_{s,u}
and
N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, we note that

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}\,dr

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}W_{s,r}\,dr=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}\,dr+\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,

and, by Theorem
2
, for
0\leq a\leq b
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}W_{a,r}\,dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(b-a)W_{a,b}+(b-a)H_{a,b}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Therefore, from the above equations, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle H_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\big(W_{s,u}-W_{u,t}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\big(H_{s,u}+H_{u,t}\big)\hskip 0.7113pt,

(67)

which is uncorrelated with
Z_{s,u}
and
N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[H_{s,t}\otimes Z_{s,u}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{32}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(W_{s,u}-W_{u,t}\big)^{\otimes 2}\Big]-\frac{3}{8}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,u}+H_{u,t}\big)^{\otimes 2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{32}\Big(\frac{1}{2}hI_{d}+\frac{1}{2}hI_{d}\Big)-\frac{3}{8}\Big(\frac{1}{24}hI_{d}+\frac{1}{24}hI_{d}\Big)=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[H_{s,t}\otimes N_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,u}+H_{u,t}\big)\otimes\big(H_{s,u}-H_{u,t}\big)\Big]=0.

Similarly, the variances of
Z_{s,u}
and
N_{s,t}
are both straightforward to calculate. Finally, equations (
65
) and (
66
) now follow by rearranging equations (
63
), (
64
) and (
67
).
∎

Due to the independence of
N_{s,t}:=H_{s,u}-H_{u,t}
, we obtain the following corollary:

Corollary 2
.

The random vector
n_{s,t}=\mathrm{sgn}\big(N_{s,t}\big)
is independent of
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u})
.

We will now present the main result of the section, which is a direct extension of Theorems
14
and
15
, to the case where we use
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t})
to approximate
L_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Theorem 17

(Approximation of space-space-time Lévy area using
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t})
)
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big]

(68)

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=\begin{cases}\frac{3}{5}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}-\frac{1}{8\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{30}h^{2},&\hskip 33.43198pt\text{if }\,i=j,\\[5.0pt]
\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{16\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+n_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{40\pi}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt,&\hskip 33.43198pt\text{if }\,i\neq j,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big)

(69)

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=\begin{cases}\frac{11}{25200}h^{4}+\big(\frac{1}{720}-\frac{1}{384\pi}\big)h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{700}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}&\hskip-14.22636pt\text{if }\,i=j,\\[3.0pt]
\,\,\,\,-\frac{1}{320\sqrt{6\pi}}W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{7}{2}},&\\[5.0pt]
\big(\frac{1}{2880}-\frac{1}{1600\pi^{2}}\big)h^{4}+\big(\frac{17}{46080}-\frac{1}{1536\pi}\big)h^{3}\big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\big)&\hskip-14.22636pt\text{if }\,i\neq j,\\[3.0pt]
\,\,\,\,+\frac{1}{1792}h^{3}\big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)-\frac{1}{640\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(1-\frac{2}{\pi}\big)\big(W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}+W_{s,t}^{j}n_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{7}{2}}.&\end{cases}

Proof.

By Corollary
1
we have that, for
0\leq a\leq b
,

\displaystyle\hskip-1.42262pt\int_{a}^{b}W_{a,r}^{i}W_{a,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr=\frac{1}{3}(b-a)W_{a,b}^{i}W_{a,b}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}(b-a)\big(W_{a,b}^{i}H_{a,b}^{j}+H_{a,b}^{i}W_{a,b}^{j}\big)+2L_{a,b}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(70)

Letting
u:=\frac{1}{2}(s+t)
denote the midpoint of
s
and
t
, we can express the integral (
70
) on the interval
[s,t]
in terms of the same integral over the half-intervals
[s,u]
and
[u,t]
.

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}^{i}W_{s,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}^{i}W_{s,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr+\int_{u}^{t}\big(W_{s,u}^{i}+W_{u,r}^{i}\big)\big(W_{s,u}^{j}+W_{u,r}^{j}\big)\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}^{i}W_{s,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr+\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{i}W_{u,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr

(71)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}+W_{s,u}^{i}\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr+W_{s,u}^{j}\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdr.

Substituting equations (
67
) and (
70
) into the above and simplifying the terms produces

\displaystyle L_{s,t}^{ij}

\displaystyle=L_{s,u}^{ij}+L_{u,t}^{ij}+\frac{1}{24}h\big(W_{s,u}^{i}-W_{u,t}^{i}\big)\big(W_{s,u}^{j}-W_{u,t}^{j}\big)

(72)

\displaystyle\hskip 61.17342pt+\frac{1}{8}h\big(W_{s,u}^{i}H_{u,t}^{j}-H_{s,u}^{i}W_{u,t}^{j}\big)+\frac{1}{8}h\big(H_{u,t}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}-W_{u,t}^{i}H_{s,u}^{j}\big).

Reformulating equation (
72
) in terms of
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
(see Theorem
16
) gives

\displaystyle L_{s,t}^{ij}=L_{s,u}^{ij}+L_{u,t}^{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{16}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{12}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}

(73)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big).

Using Theorems
14
and
16
, we will compute the following conditional expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,u}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[L_{u,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]+\frac{9}{16}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{12}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{1}{8}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{3}{10}hH_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{1}{120}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij}+\frac{3}{10}hH_{u,t}^{i}H_{u,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{120}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij}+\frac{9}{16}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{12}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{1}{8}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{3}{10}h\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)+\frac{9}{16}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{3}{10}h\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)+\frac{1}{60}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{12}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{1}{8}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{15}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{3}{20}hN_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{60}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij}

(74)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{20}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big).

Since
n_{s,t}^{i}:=\mathrm{sgn}(N_{s,t}^{i})
and
N_{s,t}^{i}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{12}h\big)
, it follows that
|N_{s,t}^{i}|
has a half-normal distribution and is independent of
n_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt
. Moreover, this implies that its moments are

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6\pi}}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\hskip-7.11317pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{3}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6\sqrt{6\pi}}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{3}{2}},

(75)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}h\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\hskip-7.11317pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{48}h^{2}.

(76)

Explicit formulae for the first four central moments of the half-normal distribution are given in
(
Elandt, 1961
, Equation (16))
. By the independence of
N_{s,t}
and
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u})
, taking the conditional expectation of equation (
73
), and applying the tower law, gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113ptL_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113ptL_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\big]\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{15}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{3}{20}hN_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{60}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{20}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{15}h\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}\big]+\frac{3}{20}h\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\big]+\frac{1}{60}h^{2}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{16\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+n_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{30}h^{2}\delta_{ij}-\frac{1}{16\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+n_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1-\delta_{ij}}{40\pi}h\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Using the decomposition (
71
) along with the independence of
W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}
and the independence of the Brownian arch processes over
[s,u]
and
[u,t]
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}^{i}W_{s,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr\,\Big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}^{i}W_{s,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr+\frac{1}{2}hW_{s,u}^{i}W_{s,u}^{j}+W_{s,u}^{i}\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr+W_{s,u}^{j}\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{i}W_{u,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr\,\Big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}^{i}W_{s,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr\,\Big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\bigg)+\mathrm{Var}\bigg(\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}^{i}W_{u,r}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptdr\,\Big|\,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\bigg).

Therefore, by Corollary
1
and Theorem
15
, the conditional variance of
L_{s,t}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big)

(77)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,u}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\big)+\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{u,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=V_{s,u}^{ij}+V_{u,t}^{ij}+\frac{1}{11520}h^{3}\Big(\big(W_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{u,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,u}^{j}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{u,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 73.26584pt+\frac{1}{11200}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{u,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,u}^{j}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{u,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big),

where
\,V_{s,u}^{ij}+V_{u,t}^{ij}=\begin{cases}\frac{11}{201600}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i=j\\[3.0pt]
\frac{1}{7200}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i\neq j\end{cases}
. Taking an expectation of (
77
) produces

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big)\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big]-\big(V_{s,u}^{ij}+V_{u,t}^{ij}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{11520}h^{3}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}^{i}+\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}^{i}+Z_{s,u}^{i}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}^{i}-\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}^{i}-Z_{s,u}^{i}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}^{j}-Z_{s,u}^{j}\bigg)^{2}\,\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{11200}h^{3}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{i}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{i}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}^{j}-\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)^{2}\,\bigg)\hskip 0.7113pt\Big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{11520}h^{3}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{4}h\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{11200}h^{3}\bigg(\frac{7}{48}h+\frac{1}{8}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{28800}h^{4}+\frac{1}{23040}h^{3}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{9}{22400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big).

Thus, the expectation of the variance (
77
) conditional on the triple
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t})
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big)\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\,\widehat{V}_{s,t}^{ij}+\frac{1}{23040}h^{3}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{9}{22400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big),

where
\,\widehat{V}_{s,t}^{ij}:=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{11200}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i=j\\[3.0pt]
\frac{1}{5760}\hskip 0.7113pth^{4},&\text{ if }i\neq j\end{cases}
. To simplify calculations, we will first assume
i=j
.

Using equation (
74
) along with the half-normal moments (
75
) and (
76
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{3}{5}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{15}h\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{3}{20}h\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{60}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{10}hH_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{i}-\frac{1}{8}h\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{i}\bigg)^{2}\,\Big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}+\frac{2}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]+\frac{9}{50}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{50}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{3}{25}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{3}\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s,u}^{i}\big]-\frac{3}{20}h^{2}W_{s,t}^{i}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{225}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{4}\Big]+\frac{1}{50}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]+\frac{1}{450}h^{3}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{75}h^{2}H_{s,t}^{i}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{3}\Big]-\frac{1}{60}h^{2}W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{9}{400}\hskip 0.7113pth^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]+\frac{1}{200}h^{3}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{3}{100}h^{2}H_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s,u}^{i}\big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]-\frac{3}{80}hW_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{3}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]+\frac{1}{3600}h^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{300}h^{3}H_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s,u}^{i}\big]-\frac{1}{240}h^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]+\frac{1}{100}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{40}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[Z_{s,u}^{i}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]+\frac{1}{64}h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{7}{4800}h^{4}+\frac{1}{768}h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{13}{320}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{3}{20\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{5}{2}}-\frac{11}{960\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{7}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt.

We can now compute the second moment of
L_{s,t}^{ij}
conditional on
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t})
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.38109pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big)\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big].

Using the formulae for these two terms, we can obtain the following second moment.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{7}{4800}h^{4}+\frac{1}{768}h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{13}{320}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{3}{20\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{5}{2}}-\frac{11}{960\sqrt{6\pi}}W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{11200}h^{4}+\frac{1}{11520}h^{3}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{11200}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{13}{8400}h^{4}+\frac{1}{720}h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{29}{700}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{3}{20\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{5}{2}}-\frac{11}{960\sqrt{6\pi}}W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{7}{2}}.

From these moments, we can now compute the conditional variance (
69
) when
i=j
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]-\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113ptL_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big]\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{13}{8400}h^{4}+\frac{1}{720}h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{29}{700}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{4}-\frac{11}{960\sqrt{6\pi}}W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{3}{20\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{5}{2}}-\Big(\frac{3}{5}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}-\frac{1}{8\sqrt{6\pi}}W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{30}h^{2}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{11}{25200}h^{4}+\bigg(\frac{1}{720}-\frac{1}{384\pi}\bigg)h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{700}h^{3}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}-\frac{1}{320\sqrt{6\pi}}W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}h^{\frac{7}{2}}.

For the remainder for the proof, we will consider the more involved case where
i\neq j
. However, our strategy to computing the conditional variance (
69
) remains unchanged.
We first derive the conditional expectations for the second conditional moment of
L_{s,t}^{ij}\hskip 0.7113pt
.
Here, we shall simplify the expansion using the independence and unbiasedness of
Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{15}hZ_{s,u}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+\frac{3}{20}hN_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{1}{20}h\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)\bigg)^{2}\,\Big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\frac{3}{20}hN_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)^{2}\Big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{400}h^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(H_{s,t}^{i}Z_{s,u}^{j}+Z_{s,u}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}\Big]+\frac{1}{225}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z_{s,u}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(Z_{s,u}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{57600}h^{4}+\bigg(\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{16}h\big(W_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}+N_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)+\frac{3}{20}hN_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{6400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big).

Hence, taking the expectation of the above conditional on
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\Big]\,\Big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{57600}h^{4}+\frac{1}{6400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{3}{40}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]-\frac{3}{40}h^{2}W_{s,t}^{j}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{9}{50}h^{2}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]+\frac{1}{256}h^{2}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{128}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}N_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]-\frac{3}{160}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{256}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]-\frac{3}{160}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{9}{400}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(N_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{5760}h^{4}+\frac{1}{3072}h^{2}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{6400}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}-\frac{3}{40\sqrt{6\pi}}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{j}+W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}\big)h^{\frac{5}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{768\pi}h^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{3}{100\pi}h^{3}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}n_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{640\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}+W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{7}{2}}.

Thus, the second moment of
L_{s,t}^{ij}
conditional on the triple
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big]^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\big)\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{2880}h^{4}+\frac{17}{46080}h^{2}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{1792}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}-\frac{3}{40\sqrt{6\pi}}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{j}+W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}\big)h^{\frac{5}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{768\pi}h^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{3}{100\pi}h^{3}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}n_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{640\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}+W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{7}{2}}.

Finally, with these moments, we can compute the conditional variance (
69
) when
i\neq j
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(L_{s,t}^{ij}\big)^{2}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\Big]-\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\hskip 0.7113ptL_{s,t}^{ij}\,\big|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,n_{s,t}\big]\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{2880}h^{4}+\frac{17}{46080}h^{2}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{1792}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{9}{25}h^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}-\frac{3}{40\sqrt{6\pi}}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{j}+W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}\big)h^{\frac{5}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{768\pi}h^{3}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+\frac{3}{100\pi}h^{3}H_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}n_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\frac{1}{640\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}+W_{s,t}^{j}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\bigg(\frac{3}{5}hH_{s,t}^{i}H_{s,t}^{j}-\frac{1}{16\sqrt{6\pi}}\big(W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}+n_{s,t}^{i}W_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{40\pi}h^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{j}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\bigg(\frac{1}{2880}-\frac{1}{1600\pi^{2}}\bigg)h^{4}+\bigg(\frac{17}{46080}-\frac{1}{1536\pi}\bigg)h^{3}\Big(\big(W_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(W_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{1792}h^{3}\Big(\big(H_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}+\big(H_{s,t}^{j}\big)^{2}\Big)-\frac{1}{640\sqrt{6\pi}}\bigg(1-\frac{2}{\pi}\bigg)\big(W_{s,t}^{i}n_{s,t}^{i}+W_{s,t}^{j}n_{s,t}^{j}\big)h^{\frac{7}{2}}.

∎

To conclude this section, we present an estimator of
K_{s,t}
which was used with the approximation (
68
) of
L_{s,t}
to design a high order splitting method
(
Foster et al., 2024
, Example 4.7)
.

Theorem 18
.

The space-time-time Lévy area
K_{s,t}
can be approximated using
n_{s,t}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}\,|\,n_{s,t}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}h^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 3.55658pt

(78)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\,|\,n_{s,t}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{720}-\frac{1}{384\pi}\bigg)\hskip 0.7113pth\hskip 0.7113pt.\hskip 3.55658pt

(79)

Proof.

We first consider the expectation of
K_{s,t}
conditional on
W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
,
which will be straightforward to compute from the definition of
K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt
, see equation (
13
).

\displaystyle h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[K_{s,t}\,\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{s}^{t}\bigg(W_{s,r}-\frac{r-s}{h}\,W_{s,t}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}h-(r-s)\bigg)dr\hskip 0.7113pt\Big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{2}h\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}\,dr-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}(r-s)\hskip 0.7113ptdr\hskip 0.7113pt\Big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]+\frac{1}{12}h^{2}W_{s,t}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{5}{12}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}H_{s,t}-\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Big[W_{s,r}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big](r-s)\hskip 0.7113ptdr.

Using the independence of
\{W_{s,r}\}_{r\in[s,u]}
and
\{W_{u,r}\}_{r\in[u,t]}
, along with the quadratic polynomial expansion of Brownian motion (see
(
Foster et al., 2024
, Theorem 2.4)
with
n=2
), we have

\displaystyle h^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}H_{s,t}-\int_{s}^{u}\mathbb{E}\Big[W_{s,r}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\,W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big](r-s)\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\int_{u}^{t}\Big(W_{s,u}+\mathbb{E}\Big[W_{u,r}\hskip 0.7113pt\big|\,W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]\Big)(r-s)\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}H_{s,t}-\int_{s}^{u}\bigg(\frac{2(r-s)}{h}W_{s,u}+\frac{24(r-s)(u-r)}{h^{2}}H_{s,u}\bigg)(r-s)\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{3}{8}h^{2}W_{s,u}-\int_{u}^{t}\bigg(\frac{2(r-u)}{h}W_{u,t}+\frac{24(r-u)(t-r)}{h^{2}}H_{u,t}\bigg)(r-s)\hskip 0.7113ptdr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}h^{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}H_{s,t}-\frac{1}{12}h^{2}W_{s,u}-\frac{1}{8}h^{2}H_{s,u}-\frac{3}{8}h^{2}W_{s,u}-\frac{5}{24}h^{2}W_{u,t}-\frac{3}{8}h^{2}H_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Expressing the above in terms of
W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}
(see Theorem
16
), it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\frac{1}{3}W_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}H_{s,t}-\frac{11}{24}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}+\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}+Z_{s,u}\bigg)-\frac{1}{8}\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}+\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{5}{24}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}W_{s,t}-\frac{3}{2}H_{s,t}-Z_{s,u}\bigg)-\frac{3}{8}\bigg(\frac{1}{4}H_{s,t}-\frac{1}{2}Z_{s,u}-\frac{1}{2}N_{s,t}\bigg)=\frac{1}{8}h^{2}N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

It follows by the tower law and the conditional expectation of
N_{s,t}
(equation (
75
)) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt|\,W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]\hskip 0.7113pt|\,n_{s,t}\big]=\frac{1}{8}\mathbb{E}\big[N_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt|\,n_{s,t}\big]=\frac{1}{8\sqrt{6\pi}}\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}h^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Finally, we note that each
\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}
will remain unchanged if
W
is replaced by
-W
,

whereas
n_{s,t}=\mathrm{sgn}(H_{s,u}-H_{u,t})
changes sign when the Brownian motion is “flipped”.
Thus, by the symmetry of
W
, the random variables
\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}
and
n_{s,t}
are uncorrelated.
So using the law of total expectation, we can write
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}n_{s,t}\big]
and
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]
as

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}n_{s,t}\Big]}_{=\,0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}=1\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[-\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}=-1\big],

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\Big]}_{=\,\frac{1}{720}h}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}=1\hskip 0.7113pt\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}=-1\hskip 0.7113pt\Big].

Solving this system gives
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}=1\hskip 0.7113pt\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}=-1\hskip 0.7113pt\big]=\frac{1}{720}h
and
\mathrm{Var}\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\,|\,n_{s,t}\big)=\mathbb{E}\big[\big(K_{s,t}^{i}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptn_{s,t}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[K_{s,t}\,\big|\,n_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt\big]^{2}=\frac{1}{720}h-\frac{1}{384\pi}h
, as required.
∎

5
Numerical examples

In this section, we will present several numerical examples demonstrating the accuracy achievable when integrals in the Brownian signature are used for SDE simulation.
These numerical examples were taken from the literature
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
;
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
;
Foster et al. (2021)
;
Foster and
Jelinčič (2024)
;
Tubikanec et al. (2022)
, so will be briefly presented, and we refer the reader to these papers for further details.

5.1
Simulating Langevin dynamics using Gaussian integrals

The underdamped Langevin diffusion (ULD) is given the following system of SDEs,

\displaystyle dx_{t}

\displaystyle=v_{t}\hskip 0.7113ptdt,\hskip 21.33955ptdv_{t}=-\gamma v_{t}\hskip 0.7113ptdt-\nabla f(x_{t})\hskip 0.7113ptdt+\sqrt{2\gamma}\hskip 0.7113ptdW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(80)

where
x,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
represent the position and momentum of a particle,
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a scalar potential,
\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt
is a friction parameter and
W
is a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion.

Under mild conditions on
f
, the SDE (
80
) is known to admit an ergodic strong solution with a stationary distribution
\pi(x,v)\propto e^{-f(x)}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\|v\|^{2}}

(
Pavliotis, 2014
, Proposition 6.1)
.
As a result, there has been great interest in the application of ULD as a Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) algorithm for high-dimensional sampling
Cheng et al. (2018)
;
Chen et al. (2014)
;
Chada et al. (2024)
;
Dalalyan and
Riou-Durand (2020)
;
Riou-Durand et al. (2023)
;
Scott et al. (2025)
.

Due to the structure of (
80
), it is possible for SDE solvers to achieve a third order convergence rate by using
(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,K_{s,t})
. Currently, the following solver is the only to exhibit such fast convergence whilst only requiring evaluations of the gradient
\nabla f
,

Definition 14

(The SORT
2
2

2

S
hifted
O
DE with
R
unge-Kutta
T
hree
method for Underdamped Langevin Dynamics
Foster et al. (2024)
;
Foster et al. (2021)
)
.

\displaystyle V_{n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=V_{n}+\sqrt{2\gamma}\,(H_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}+6K_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}),

\displaystyle X_{n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=X_{n}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)V_{n}^{(1)}-\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)\nabla f(X_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 29.87538pt+\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}h_{n}}\bigg)\sqrt{2\gamma}\,\big(W_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}-12K_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}\big),

\displaystyle X_{n+1}

\displaystyle:=X_{n}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)V_{n}^{(1)}-\bigg(\frac{e^{-\gamma h_{n}}+\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{3}\nabla f(X_{n})+\frac{2}{3}\nabla f\big(X_{n}^{(1)}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 29.87538pt+\bigg(\frac{e^{-\gamma h_{n}}+\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}h_{n}}\bigg)\sqrt{2\gamma}\,\big(W_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}-12K_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}\big),

\displaystyle V_{n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=e^{-\gamma h_{n}}V_{n}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{6}e^{-\gamma h_{n}}\nabla f(X_{n})h_{n}-\frac{2}{3}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}\nabla f\big(X_{n}^{(1)}\big)h_{n}-\frac{1}{6}\nabla f(X_{n+1})h_{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 29.87538pt+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma h_{n}}\bigg)\sqrt{2\gamma}\,\big(W_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}-12K_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}\big),

\displaystyle V_{n+1}

\displaystyle:=V_{n}^{(2)}-\sqrt{2\gamma}\,(H_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}-6K_{t_{n},\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}),

where
h_{n}:=t_{n+1}-t_{n}>0\hskip 0.7113pt
denotes the step size and
(X_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,V_{n})
approximates
(x_{t_{n}}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{t_{n}})
.

Remark 13
.

Since
\nabla f(X_{n+1})
can be used in the next step to compute
(X_{n+2},V_{n+2})
, the SORT method only uses two additional evaluations of the gradient
\nabla f
per step.
Hence, this is an example of the “First Same As Last” property in numerical analysis.
Moreover, as evaluating
\nabla f
is typically much more computationally expensive than generating
d
-dimensional Gaussian random vectors in practice, we would expect that the SORT method is about twice as expensive per step as the Euler-Maruyama method.

Firstly, to demonstrate the third order strong convergence of the SORT method, we present an experiment in
Foster et al. (2021)
;
Foster et al. (2024)
where
f
comes from a Bayesian logistic regression.
This involves German credit data from
Lichman (2013)
, where each of the
m=1000
individuals has
d=49
features
x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a label
y_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
indicating if they are creditworthy.
The Bayesian logistic regression model states that
\mathbb{P}(Y_{i}=y_{i}|x_{i})=(1+e^{-y_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\theta})^{-1}

where
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are parameters coming from the target density
\pi(\theta)\propto\exp(-f(\theta))
with

\displaystyle f(\theta)=\delta\|\theta\|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\log\big(1+\exp\big(-y_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\theta\big)\big)\hskip 0.7113pt.

In the experiment, the regularisation parameter is
\delta=0.05
, the friction coefficient is
\gamma=2
and the initial parameter configuration
\theta_{0}
is sampled from a Gaussian prior as

\displaystyle\theta_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,10I_{d}\big).

We will use a fixed time horizon of
T=1000
and compute the following error estimator:

Definition 15

(
Strong error estimator
)
.

For
N\geq 1
, let
\big\{\theta_{k}^{h}\big\}_{0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq k\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptN}
denote a numerical solution of (
80
) computed over
[0,T]
at times
t_{k}:=kh
with step size
h=\frac{T}{N}
. Let
\big\{\theta_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\big\}_{0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq k\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pt2N}
be the approximation obtained by using a smaller step size of
\frac{1}{2}h
.
We generate
n
samples of these numerical solutions and define an estimator at time
T
,

\displaystyle S_{N,n}:=\sqrt{\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\theta_{N,i}^{h}-\theta_{2N,i}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\big\|^{2}\,}\,,

(81)

where each
\big(\theta_{N,i}^{k}\hskip 0.7113pt,\theta_{2N,i}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)
is computed from the same sample path of Brownian motion
and each initial value
\theta_{0,i}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}=\theta_{0,i}^{\frac{1}{2}h}
is sampled from the normal distribution
\mathcal{N}\big(0,10\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}\big)
.

Remark 14
.

By the law of large numbers, the estimator (
81
) converges as
n\rightarrow\infty
to

\displaystyle S_{N}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\theta_{N}^{h}\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}-\theta_{2N}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\big\|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

almost surely. Though for large
h
,
S_{N}
may not be close to the L2 error
\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\|\theta_{N}^{h}-\theta_{\hskip 0.7113ptT}\|^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\hskip-2.84526pt
.

Using this estimator, we shall compare SORT against several prominent schemes. Whilst the literature is extensive, we chose the UBU splitting
Sanz-Serna and
Zygalakis (2021)
;
Chada et al. (2024)
, randomized midpoint method
Shen and Lee (2019)
, OBABO splitting
Bussi and Parrinello (2007)
;
Riou-Durand et al. (2023)
and exponential Euler scheme
Cheng et al. (2018)
.

The results of the above numerical experiment are presented in Figure
6
.
and code for this experiment can be found at
github.com/james-m-foster/high-order-langevin
.

Figure 6:
L2 error estimated for (
80
) with
n=1,000
sample paths as a function of step size
h=\frac{T}{N}
.

From this graph, we see that SORT converges faster than its nearest competitor (the UBU splitting) and is an order of magnitude more accurate with a step size of
0.1
.
Moreover, its worth noting that this accelerated convergence was only made possible by generating the integrals in the log-signature of the Brownian motion
(W,H,K)_{t_{k}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptt_{k+1}}
.

To show the applicability of the SORT method to challenging sampling problems,
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
considers the 10-dimensional “Neal’s funnel” distribution
Neal (2003)
, which is defined as

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}(0,9),\hskip 14.22636ptY\sim\mathcal{N}(0,e^{X}I_{9}),

(82)

where
(X,Y)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{9}
, and therefore corresponds to the scalar potential
f
given by

\displaystyle f(x,y)=\frac{1}{2}x+\frac{1}{18}x^{2}+\frac{1}{2}e^{-x}\|y\|^{2}.

Neal’s funnel is known to be a challenging distribution for MCMC algorithms as it has a narrow high density region when
X<0
and a wide low density region when
X>0
. In particular, most MCMC samplers struggle to enter into the narrow “funnel” region.

In
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
, the following MCMC algorithms were tested on Neal’s funnel distribution:

•

The Euler-Maruyama and SORT methods with a constant step size of
h=\frac{1}{64}
.

•

The SORT method with an adaptive step size. Here, steps are determined using the “Proportional-Integral” (PI) controller that is built into the “Diffrax” library
Kidger ()
. As recommended by
Ilie et al. (2014)
, this PI controller used the parameters
K_{P}=0.1,K_{I}=0.3
.

•

The No U-Turn Sampler (NUTS)
Hoffman and Gelman (2014)
, which is a state-of-the-art MCMC algorithm.

For the above algorithms, samples were generated using 64 chains of 128 samples. The time between samples was
\Delta t=2
and an initial “burn-in” period of 16 iterations was used. When necessary, adaptive step sizes were shortened to produce the samples.
For the experiment’s code, see
github.com/andyElking/Single-seed_BrownianMotion
.

Figure 7:
Left: Qualitative comparison of exact samples from Neal’s funnel and approximate samples generated by different methods. Right: Graph showing estimated strong convergence rates for SORT, Euler-Maruyama and SRA1 – which is a well-known high order stochastic Runge-Kutta method
Rößler (2010)
.

To empirically compare the quality of samples produced by each MCMC algorithm, the authors of
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
normalised the samples using the rescaling:
x^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}=\frac{1}{3}x,\,\,\,y^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}=e^{-\frac{1}{2}x}y
.
Since the true funnel distribution will produce rescaled samples
(x^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime},y^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{10})
, the approximate samples were compared to
\mathcal{N}(0,I_{10})
using the following error metrics:

•

The maximum and average absolute error of the entries within the empirical mean (the correct mean is
0
) and covariance matrix (the correct covariance matrix is
I_{10}
).

•

The average Effective Sample Size (ESS) computed from the marginal distributions.

•

The average Kolmogorov-Smirnov (KS) test
p\hskip 0.7113pt
-value computed from each marginal.

In addition, outliers with large magnitudes (
\|\cdot\|>100
) were removed from the samples generated by the SDE solvers with constant step sizes. On the other hand, the adaptive step size controller prevented all such outliers by reducing its step size.

Table 1:
A quantitative comparison of the No U-Turn Sampler (NUTS) against Langevin-based MCMC algorithms (obtain from different discretizations of (
80
)) for sampling from Neal’s funnel. The results were averaged over 5 independent runs. We refer the reader to
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
for further details.

From Table
1
and Figure
7
, we see that the adaptive SORT method (which uses Gaussian integrals in the Brownian log-signature) gives the best overall performance. However, as an MCMC algorithm, it is worth noting that the SORT method should be viewed differently from NUTS, which is Metropolis-adjusted and thus has the correct stationary distribution. On the other hand, the SORT method can explore the parameter space faster as well as take smaller step sizes in the more difficult regions.
Moreover, the additional bias introduced by SORT comes from its discretization error, which quickly decreases at a third order rate (as is evident in both Figures
6
and
7
).

In addition to these results, further experiments comparing a SORT-based method to NUTS
Hoffman and Gelman (2014)
for performing Bayesian logistic regression on real-world datasets can be found in
Jelinčič
et al. (2024)
and
Scott et al. (2025)
. Whilst SORT-based Langevin MCMC is a topic of future work, we can already note that it gives a clear application of space-time-time Lévy area (
13
).

5.2
Space-space Lévy area examples

In this section, we present the experiments from
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
;
Foster and
Jelinčič (2024)
to demonstrate the Lévy area approximations considered in section
3
.
Firstly, in
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
, several metrics were computed to compare the distributions of Brownian Lévy area and the weak approximation (
42
).

5.2.1
Lévy area of a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion (with
d\leq 4
)

In the first experiment of
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
, the authors compared four methods for approximately generating the Lévy area of Brownian motion over
[0,1]
given the path increment
W_{1}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

•

The weak Lévy area approximation based on matching moments (Definition
7
).

•

The “LévyGAN” model – trained according to the methodology proposed in
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
.

•

The Lévy area approximation of Davie
Davie (2014)
, which is based on matching moments.

•

The truncated Fourier series approximation of Lévy area implemented in Julia
Kastner and
Rößler (2023)
.

As the “true” samples,
2^{20}
independent tuples
(W_{1}^{1}\hskip 0.7113pt,W_{1}^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt,A_{0,1}^{12})
of increments and finely discretized Lévy areas were generated using the Fourier series approximation.
Following this,
2^{20}
independent samples were generated using each method (
d=2
) and the empirical
2
-Wasserstein error was computed between the two sets of samples.
The truncation level of the Fourier series was chosen so that it produced samples that gave a comparable
2
-Wasserstein error to the weak Lévy area and LévyGAN samples.
This generation was done using a GPU (NVIDIA Quadro RTX 8000), and we refer the reader to the appendix of
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
for further details regarding this numerical experiment.
In particular, the associated code is available at
github.com/andyElking/LevyGAN
.

)

, we see the weak approximation (
42
) achieves similar accuracy as the LévyGAN model (in under half the time) and outperforms other methods when
d=2
. However, in the
d=2
case, there is an algorithm for exactly generating Lévy area
Gaines and Lyons (1994)
. Therefore, further numerical experiments were conducted in
Jelinčič
et al. (2025)
for testing the joint distribution of the approximate Lévy areas in multidimensional cases where
d>2
.
These test metrics consist of Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) distances with two different kernels (polynomial and Gaussian), an Empirical Unitary Characteristic Function Distance (EUCFD) and computing the largest error in the fourth moments. As before, we refer the reader to

and its appendix for further experimental details.

Fourth moment, MMD and characteristic function based test metrics across different methods for approximating the Lévy area of a
